# Codebase review (2026-11-13)

Found by 15 Haiku reviewers, then triaged. Sorted by impact on the user (P1 first); item numbers are the original ones (1.x visible bugs, 2.x core/server, 3.x rules, 4.x dead code, 5.x duplication, 6.x performance, 7.x docs). Work top to bottom.

Tags: **[checked]** = confirmed by reading the code. **[unverified]** = reviewer's claim, not yet confirmed (check before fixing). **[rejected]** = wrong, kept so it isn't re-raised.

Mark items `[x]` as they are fixed, `[-]` if dropped.

Under each item, a second pass (Sonnet, read-only) re-checked the claim and proposed the simplest fix, preferring ones that delete code:
- **Verdict:** confirmed / partly / rejected / leave (real but not worth fixing) / keep (not dead: used or documented API). Where it disagrees with the tag on the item line, the Verdict is the newer read.
- **Fix:** the proposed change. These are sketches and have not been compiled.
- **Lines:** rough net change.

Tally: 134 confirmed, 59 partly, 26 rejected, 16 leave, 3 keep. Corrections from the second pass: 1.39 is in `overlays.lua:155`, not popups.lua; 1.25 also affects the GUI (fix in bone-ui `find_chat`); 7.1 is rejected (`/help` embeds usage.md and protocol.md through `bone-ui/src/lua/state.rs`); several Lua exports in 4.17 are used by plugins in `~/projects/bone-catalog` and stay.

A third pass (Sonnet, read-only) checked each fix for regressions: every caller, the TUI and desktop sharing bone-ui, plugins in bone-catalog, session/config compatibility, tests that pin output.
- **Risk:** what the fix could break. **Benefit:** what it buys.
- **Call:** do / do with care (what to check) / revise (the fix is wrong or incomplete; a safer version is given) / skip (not worth the risk) / n/a (verdict was rejected/leave/keep).

Tally: 69 do, 49 care, 34 revise, 34 skip, 52 n/a. Cross-cutting findings:
- `bone3/plugins/files` is a stale v1.0.0 copy; the live plugin is bone-catalog 3.8.1, which already has the 1.9 decode fix and an opener check. 1.8–1.10 and 2.88–2.95 belong in the catalog copy (or the stale copy should be deleted).
- LuaJIT has no `utf8` library: fixes that call `utf8.*` (1.38, 1.48) throw at runtime.
- `background.duration` is also used by the TUI tray (`ui/tray.lua:21,232`), so 1.15/5.1 must not delete it; bone-tui tests pin "12.0k".
- Fixes touching crates/bone-gui (1.49, 2.85, parts of others) need approval first under the Lua-only rule.
- 6.1's shared reqwest client is safe for openai.rs and wait.rs, not health.rs (different tokio runtimes).

---

## P1 — crashes, hangs, lost or wrong data

Things a user hits in normal use that abort, hang, lose text or send the wrong thing.

- [x] 2.1 [checked] `crates/bone-core/src/scripting.rs:1189` — every `bone.session.*` yield recurses `self.step(...)`; long Lua loops overflow the 2 MB Lua thread stack and abort the core. Loop instead.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `step` calls itself for every `{wait={session=...}}` yield (scripting.rs ~1189), one Rust frame per session call.
  - **Fix:** Make `step` a loop: `let mut args = args; loop { let r = thread.resume(args); ... }`. In the session branch set `args = <converted values>` (on conversion error `return self.finish(done, Err(e))`) and `continue` instead of `self.step(...)`; all other branches already `return`.
  - **Lines:** about -4/+5
  - **Risk:** low — scripting.rs `step` callers (cancelled(), Job::Resume, run) all pass owned thread/args, so a loop is a drop-in; every non-session branch already `return`s. Only new behavior: a Lua loop that calls bone.session.* forever (without yielding elsewhere) now spins the Lua thread instead of crashing it; it was already fatal. No test/golden pins recursion.
  - **Benefit:** medium — removes a core-aborting stack overflow for plugins looping over bone.session.*.
  - **Call:** do — make `args` mutable and `continue`; keep the 2.21 change separate (see 2.21).
- [x] 2.61 [checked] `crates/bone-lua/src/lib.rs:756` — `from_lua` has no cycle check; `t.self = t` then encode aborts the process.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `from_lua` recurses on tables with no depth or visited check; `t.self = t` overflows the stack and aborts the process.
  - **Fix:** rename the body to `fn walk(v: &Value, depth: u32)`, return `Err(mlua::Error::runtime("table nests too deeply (a cycle?)"))` when `depth > 128`, recurse with `depth + 1`, and keep `pub fn from_lua(v) { walk(v, 0) }`. (`bone.json.encode` goes through mlua serde, which already detects recursion.)
  - **Lines:** about -0/+8
  - **Risk:** low — depth limit 128 only affects cycles/pathological nesting; serde_json decode (bone.json.decode) already caps at 128, so legitimate round-trips fit if the check is `depth > 128` starting at 0. ~30 callers (scripting.rs, bone-ui lua.rs, runtime.rs) just propagate the new Err. Shared (non-cyclic) DAGs still convert fine.
  - **Benefit:** medium — a plugin `t.self = t` currently aborts the whole core/TUI process with a stack overflow.
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 2.4 [checked] `crates/bone-core/src/agent.rs:337` — Lua system prompt awaited without racing the cancel token.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `s.system_prompt(...).await` in `Turn::drive` (agent.rs ~337) has no `select!` against `self.cancel`, unlike `hooks()`, `compact()` and the provider call.
  - **Fix:** Same pattern as `hooks()`: `tokio::select! { r = s.system_prompt(&cwd, &self.session_id) => Some(r.map_err(Stop::Failed)?), _ = self.cancel.cancelled() => { s.cancel_session(&self.session_id); return Err(Stop::Cancelled); } }`.
  - **Lines:** about -5/+9
  - **Risk:** low — Turn::drive is the only caller; mirrors hooks() exactly (select on self.cancel, cancel_session, return Stop::Cancelled). The user message is already recorded and TurnStarted already emitted before this point, so a cancelled turn here ends via the normal Cancelled outcome path (turn_end hook + TurnFinished still run). Dropping the Done::Prompt oneshot is harmless (reply.send ignored). Cancel_session targets only this session's coroutines.
  - **Benefit:** medium — a system_prompt fn that does bone.ask/http/sleep currently makes cancel hang the turn.
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 2.5 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/lib.rs:162` / `agent.rs:92` — a panic in `run_turn` leaves the session busy forever (no turn/finished).
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — no `catch_unwind`, no `panic = "abort"`; tokio swallows the task panic, so `active` is never cleared and `TurnFinished` never sent.
  - **Fix:** In `run_turn` wrap the drive: `match AssertUnwindSafe(turn.drive(text, images)).catch_unwind().await { Ok(Ok(())) => Completed, Ok(Err(..)) => ..existing.., Err(_) => TurnOutcome::Failed { message: "internal error: the turn panicked".into() } }` (`futures_util::FutureExt` is already a dependency). Rest of run_turn then cleans up normally.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+4
  - **Risk:** medium — futures_util FutureExt is available and no panic=abort exists, so catch_unwind works, but a panic while holding the session std Mutex poisons it and the cleanup right after (`session.lock().unwrap()` in run_turn, and every later request on that session) panics again, so the fix only helps panics outside the session/inner locks. It also converts crashes that currently surface loudly into a Failed turn (message hides the panic payload; log it with eprintln/tracing). `safe_point` may be left true if the panic was inside safe_hooks; reset it in the Err arm.
  - **Benefit:** medium — a turn panic (e.g. provider/Lua edge case) no longer wedges the session as busy forever.
  - **Call:** do with care — include the panic payload in the message/log, reset s.safe_point in the panic arm, and use `unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)` for the cleanup locks in run_turn if you want it actually robust.
- [-] 2.53 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/provider/openai.rs:101` — no idle timeout mid-stream; a stalled server hangs the turn. **Dropped:** the model call already races cancel (agent.rs), so Esc ends a stall; a read timeout would also fail slow local prompt eval.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `http_client()` only sets `connect_timeout`; nothing bounds a silent mid-stream stall.
  - **Fix:** add `.read_timeout(Duration::from_secs(300))` to the builder in `http_client()` (reqwest 0.12 per-read idle timeout; error surfaces through the existing `stream error:` map_err). Generous value so slow local prompt-eval before first byte still works. Anthropic shares the client.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+2
  - **Risk:** medium — reqwest 0.12.28 read_timeout also bounds time-to-first-headers, not just mid-stream gaps. llama.cpp/ollama/tabbyAPI with a huge prompt on slow hardware (or non-streaming-reasoning models) can send zero bytes for > 5 min; this now fails the turn, and `send()` treats `e.is_timeout()` as retryable, so it restarts the multi-minute prompt eval up to 3 times. The same client is shared by LuaProvider (scripting.rs:676) and `BuiltIn` for Lua `prepare`d requests.
  - **Benefit:** medium — a hung/stalled server no longer wedges a turn forever.
  - **Call:** do with care — use a much larger idle bound (>= 15-30 min) or make it a provider option (`options.timeout`), and do not retry a read timeout in send() (only connect timeouts).
- [x] 2.44 [checked] `crates/bone-core/src/tools/shell.rs:281` — `wait()` checks state before creating `notified()`; a finish in between is lost and deadline-less waits hang.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `ProcessRegistry::wait` checks state, then creates `process.done.notified()`; `finish_job` calls `notify_waiters()`, which only wakes `Notified` futures that already exist, so a finish between the check and the creation is lost and a deadline-less wait hangs.
  - **Fix:** Move `let notified = process.done.notified();` above the state check in the loop (a `Notified` created before `notify_waiters` is woken even if unpolled). Also lock the snapshot once: `let s = process.snapshot.lock().unwrap(); if s.state != Running { return Ok(s.clone()) }`.
  - **Lines:** about -1/+1
  - **Risk:** low — none found. finish_job sets state under the snapshot lock then notify_waiters(); a Notified created before the check is woken even if unpolled, so ordering is correct. select!/notified.await usage unchanged. Tests (tools/tests.rs shell tests) unaffected.
  - **Benefit:** medium — fixes a real lost-wakeup that hangs deadline-less `wait` on a fast-finishing job.
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 1.7 [checked] `crates/bone-gui/src/lib.rs:510` — `unsent()` drops the failed text if the draft is non-empty, and restores into the current chat instead of chat `buf`.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `unsent` (lib.rs:509) writes to `self.draft`/`self.images` (the chat on screen) regardless of `buf`, and discards `text` if the current draft is non-empty.
  - **Fix:** pick the slot of chat `buf`: `let (draft, imgs) = if buf == self.current { (&mut self.draft, &mut self.images) } else { let d = self.drafts.entry(buf).or_default(); (&mut d.0, &mut d.1) };` then `*draft = if draft.is_empty() { text } else { format!("{text}\n{draft}") }; imgs.splice(0..0, images);` (borrow of `self.error` after, as now).
  - **Lines:** about -4/+8
  - **Risk:** medium — if buf is the current chat, the code at lib.rs:509 sets self.draft with no cursor_to_end, draft_epoch or prompt_changed, so the restored text can show with the caret at 0 and the prompt/marks cache stale. Merging `text\ndraft` changes the old "discard when the draft is non-empty" behavior (safer, but new). For a non-current buf, the entry must use the widened 1.5 tuple. Callers: lib.rs:461 and 495 only.
  - **Benefit:** medium — a failed send in chat A no longer dumps A's text into chat B's draft.
  - **Call:** do with care — after 1.5 land the tuple change first; for buf == current also call cursor_to_end() and prompt_changed(); test: send in A, switch to B, force the send error, confirm A's draft restored and B untouched.
- [x] 1.5 [checked] `crates/bone-gui/src/prompt.rs:22` — `prompt.pastes` is window-wide but drafts are per chat (`chats.rs:226` swaps only draft + images). Sending or clearing in one chat wipes others' pastes, so a parked draft sends a literal `[Pasted text #n]`, and numbering (`pastes.len()+1`) can reuse a token. Move pastes into the per-chat draft.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `prompt.pastes` is window-wide while show_chat (chats.rs:226) swaps only draft + images; `clear_draft` (lib.rs:504) and `set_prompt_text` (lua.rs:531) clear it for all chats.
  - **Fix:** widen the per-chat tuple instead of adding a struct: `drafts: HashMap<usize, (String, Vec<ImageAttachment>, Vec<(String, String)>)>` (lib.rs:140), and in show_chat swap `self.prompt.pastes` along with draft/images (`std::mem::take(&mut self.prompt.pastes)` into the tuple, and back out of `drafts.remove(&buf).unwrap_or_default()`). `gui_draft` (ops.rs:161) only reads `.0` so it is unaffected. Numbering via `pastes.len()+1` is then unique per chat.
  - **Lines:** about -3/+6
  - **Risk:** low — `drafts` is touched only in chats.rs:230-231, lib.rs:140/215 and ops.rs:161 (gui_draft reads only .0). clear_draft and set_prompt_text still clear the live pastes. unsent() (1.7) must use the widened tuple. Entries for deleted chats leak a few bytes, as they already do.
  - **Benefit:** medium — a paste placeholder no longer expands to another chat's content or collides on #N.
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 2.7 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/session.rs:855` — a final line that parses but lacks `\n` is counted good; the next append glues onto it and loses a record.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `read_file` counts any line that parses as good, even without `\n`; `good_len` then includes it and the next append glues onto it.
  - **Fix:** In the `read_until` loop, right after the EOF check add `if buf.last() != Some(&b'\n') { break; }` (torn tail, truncated away later) and delete the `Err(_) if !line.ends_with('\n') => break` arm.
  - **Lines:** about -2/+3
  - **Risk:** low — the index (index.rs sync_once) already treats a final line without `\n` as still being written, so this makes read_file consistent with it. Only loss: a last record lacking `\n` (hand-edited/fixture files, or a write killed between line and `\n`; write_line writes line+`\n` in one write_all so normal files are fine) is now dropped and truncated on load (set_len at session.rs:536). Existing tests (recovers_from_torn_tail, damaged_line_mid_file) still hold; header line (read_line) is untouched.
  - **Benefit:** medium — prevents a silently glued/lost record after a crash mid-append.
  - **Call:** do — grep tests/fixtures that write jsonl without a trailing newline (bone/tests/images.rs:279, agent_tests.rs:1729/2195 only read paths).
- [x] 2.39 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/mcp/oauth.rs:884,60` — `Flow::start` outside the timeout and clients have no timeouts; `load()` returns empty on parse error and the next store erases every sign-in.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `Flow::start` is awaited outside the 600s timeout in `auth_begin`, every `reqwest::Client::new()` in oauth.rs (start ~269, `token_request` ~462) has no timeout, and `load()` does `.ok()...unwrap_or_default()` so a corrupt file reads as empty and the next `store`/`sign_out` overwrites all sign-ins.
  - **Fix:** Add `fn client() -> reqwest::Client { reqwest::Client::builder().timeout(Duration::from_secs(30)).build().unwrap_or_default() }` in oauth.rs and use it in `Flow::start` and `token_request` (that alone bounds `Flow::start`, 2.43's mutex hold, and refresh). In `load()`, on a parse failure `rename(p, p.with_extension("json.bad"))` before returning empty so the broken file is kept rather than erased.
  - **Lines:** about -2/+10
  - **Risk:** low-medium — client() 30s per-request: Flow::start does up to ~7 discovery GETs + a POST + registration sequentially, so worst case is still minutes and auth_begin (mcp.rs:~881) remains un-bounded overall; token_request/refresh bounded as intended (also bounds the ONE refresh mutex). reqwest timeout covers body read too (fine for these JSON docs). load() rename-to-.bad: load() runs on every HTTP request (post() -> access_token), so concurrent callers may both attempt the rename (second fails silently, ok); a transiently unreadable/half-written file is impossible since save() renames atomically, but on Windows a rename of a file another thread has open can fail (harmless, returns empty as before). Preserves old tokens in mcp-auth.json.bad (0600 kept by rename). No tests/Lua/catalog touch mcp-auth.json (only README prose).
  - **Benefit:** medium — prevents a corrupt/unknown-shape file from silently erasing every sign-in, and stops indefinite hangs in sign-in/refresh.
  - **Call:** revise — also wrap `oauth::Flow::start(...)` in auth_begin in an overall tokio::time::timeout (e.g. 60s) since the per-request timeout alone allows several minutes; otherwise as proposed.
- [x] 2.31 [checked] `crates/bone-core/src/mcp.rs:607` — stderr `lines()` stops at the first non-UTF-8 line; child then blocks on full stderr. Stdout reader also treats it as a dead connection (190).
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — tokio `Lines::next_line` returns InvalidData on a non-UTF-8 line; the stderr task `while let Ok(Some(..))` exits (pipe then fills and blocks the child) and the stdout reader's `Err(e) => break` reports a dead connection.
  - **Fix:** Add one small helper in mcp.rs, `async fn next_line(r: &mut (impl AsyncBufRead + Unpin)) -> io::Result<Option<String>>` that does `let mut b = Vec::new(); if r.read_until(b'\n', &mut b).await? == 0 { return Ok(None) } Ok(Some(String::from_utf8_lossy(&b).trim_end().to_owned()))`, and use it with a plain `BufReader` in both the stdout reader (LineConn::new) and the stderr tail task (~line 607) instead of `.lines()`.
  - **Lines:** about -4/+10
  - **Risk:** low — none found. Helper is only used by LineConn::new (stdout) and the stderr tail task; tests use duplex streams and bash server (tests.rs:266). Lossy decode only turns bad bytes into U+FFFD (still valid JSON string content); trim_end also trims trailing spaces which JSON ignores. Same unbounded line buffer as lines(). AsyncBufReadExt already imported.
  - **Benefit:** medium — non-UTF-8 stderr/stdout no longer kills the connection or blocks the child on a full stderr pipe (common with Windows/legacy-codepage tools).
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 2.30 [checked] `crates/bone-core/src/mcp.rs:732` — restart budget reset on every successful connect; crash-after-init loops forever at 1s.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `restarts = 0` runs on every successful connect (mcp.rs ~732), so a server that initializes then dies never exceeds restarts=1 and loops at 1s forever.
  - **Fix:** In `supervise`, drop `restarts = 0` from the `Ok` arm; record `let up = Instant::now();` there, and after the inner `loop` ends, `if up.elapsed() > Duration::from_secs(60) { restarts = 0; }` before yielding `why` (make the inner loop `let why = loop {..break why}; ...; why`). A server that stayed up a minute gets a fresh budget; a flapping one reaches MAX_RESTARTS and goes Failed.
  - **Lines:** about -1/+4
  - **Risk:** medium — behavior change: any stdio server that dies <60s after connecting (npx cold starts, flaky wrappers) now reaches MAX_RESTARTS=5 and goes Status::Failed permanently (supervise returns; only reload/reconnect revives; mcp.rs:1120 then returns None for it) where today it loops forever and recovers. HTTP SESSION_LOST (mcp.rs:401, emits ConnEvent::Closed) also counts as a crash: servers expiring sessions <60s repeatedly would end Failed. tests.rs follows_tool_changes_and_restarts_after_a_crash (one crash) and an_http_session_that_ended_is_started_again (one loss) still pass. Need `use std::time::Instant` (mcp.rs:20 imports only Duration).
  - **Benefit:** medium — stops an infinite 1s respawn loop of a crash-after-init server (CPU/log churn, process spam).
  - **Call:** do with care — keep the 60s threshold; confirm the Failed state is surfaced in the Lua/TUI mcp panel with a reconnect action (reconnect()/spawn_supervisor resets the budget); run mcp/tests.rs.
- [x] 2.66 [unverified] `crates/bone-server/src/unix.rs:85`, `pipe.rs:74` — transient accept errors end the server.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `self.listener.accept().await?` (unix.rs) and `pipe.connect().await?`/`self.instance()?` (pipe.rs) end `run_counted`, which main.rs turns into "accept failed" and a server exit on a transient EMFILE/ECONNABORTED.
  - **Fix:** in unix.rs match the accept: `Err(e) => { eprintln!("bone: accept failed: {e}"); tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(100)).await; continue }` (needs tokio `time` feature on bone-server). Do the same for `pipe.connect()` in pipe.rs; leave the `instance()?` failure fatal.
  - **Lines:** about -1/+8 per file
  - **Risk:** low-medium — persistent accept errors (EMFILE) would loop every 100 ms printing to core.log forever (log spam; core.log is opened non-append, see 2.70). On Windows pipe.rs the same retry on a broken NamedPipeServer instance can spin; replace `pipe` with a fresh `self.instance()?` on connect error before continuing. bone-server needs tokio `time` in non-dev deps (workspace tokio has it via other crates; add explicitly).
  - **Benefit:** medium — a transient EMFILE/ECONNABORTED no longer kills a shared core that other UIs depend on.
  - **Call:** do with care — rate-limit the eprintln (first + every Nth), back off, and re-create the pipe instance on Windows.
- [x] 2.51 [checked] `crates/bone-core/src/provider/openai.rs:495` — index-less tool-call deltas all map to index 0; parallel calls merge.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `tc.index.unwrap_or(pos)` uses the position inside one chunk's array, so index-less calls arriving one per chunk all land on key 0 and their args concatenate.
  - **Fix:** in `Accumulator::apply` (openai.rs ~495) when `tc.index` is None: a non-empty `tc.id` starts a new call, anything else continues the last one: `let index = tc.index.unwrap_or_else(|| match tc.id.as_deref() { Some(id) if !id.is_empty() => self.calls.keys().next_back().map_or(0, |k| k + 1), _ => self.calls.keys().next_back().copied().unwrap_or(0) });` (drops `enumerate()`/`pos`).
  - **Lines:** about -2/+6
  - **Risk:** medium — proposed rule (id present => new call, else continue last) regresses servers that omit `index` AND send several calls in one chunk with empty/absent ids (Gemini OpenAI-compat style): today `pos` separates them, the fix would merge them into one call (openai.rs Accumulator::apply). Also a server repeating the same id on every delta would be split into many calls. Existing tests (openai.rs ~574-632) all carry `index`, so none pin this.
  - **Benefit:** medium — fixes merged parallel calls from index-less servers that stream one call per chunk.
  - **Call:** revise — keep `pos` semantics as the fallback and decide "new call" per index-less delta by: id non-empty and != last call's id, OR function.name non-empty while the last call already has a name; otherwise continue the last call. Add nothing for servers that send `index`.
- [x] 2.54 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/provider/openai.rs:331` — Chat Completions replays reasoning text from any model (others gate on `reasoning_state.model`).
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `replay_field` only picks the field name from a same-model message, but `wire_message` then writes `reasoning` onto every assistant message, including ones from a previously selected model.
  - **Fix:** pass `&config.model` into `wire_messages`/`wire_message`, destructure `reasoning_state` in the Assistant arm and gate: `if let (Some(field), Some(s)) = (replay, reasoning_state) && s.model == model { m[field] = json!(reasoning); }`. Same gate Responses/Anthropic already use; update the test call sites' signature.
  - **Lines:** about -2/+5
  - **Risk:** medium — hard gate `s.model == model` drops replay for assistant messages with reasoning but no reasoning_state (older sessions, messages rebuilt via messages_from_lua/agent.rs:1041, Lua providers) and defeats `replay_reasoning = true` forcing for DeepSeek-style models that need reasoning_content echoed. Also the unit tests at openai.rs:666-695 call wire_message(msg, None/Some(..)) and need the new signature (no other callers; wire_messages/replay_field are private).
  - **Benefit:** medium — stops sending another model's reasoning to a model it wasn't produced by (400s / wasted tokens after a model switch).
  - **Call:** revise — suppress only when reasoning_state is Some and its model differs: `reasoning_state.as_ref().is_none_or(|s| s.model == model)`; keep replay when state is None.
- [x] 2.55 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/provider/openai.rs:256`, `openai_responses.rs:32` — reasoning_effort "default" forwarded (anthropic filters it). One normalize helper.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — anthropic.rs:56 filters `"default"`; openai.rs:256 and openai_responses.rs:32 only filter empty (and the settings page shows "default" for unset, so the string is a real input).
  - **Fix:** add `impl ProviderConfig { pub fn effort(&self) -> Option<&str> { self.reasoning_effort.as_deref().filter(|e| !e.is_empty() && *e != "default") } }` in config.rs and use `config.effort()` in all three request builders (anthropic's `replay` logic then reads `config.effort() != Some("none")`).
  - **Lines:** about -9/+6
  - **Risk:** low — "default" is only ever a UI placeholder (settings_page.lua:230 shows "default" for unset); no server accepts it as a real effort. Callers: openai.rs:256, openai_responses.rs:32, anthropic.rs:56 only; ProviderConfig is plain serde so an added `impl` method is safe. Per-call override (runtime.rs:180, scripting.rs:389) goes through the same field.
  - **Benefit:** medium — selecting "default" in settings currently sends `reasoning_effort:"default"` and gets a 400 on OpenAI-compatible servers.
  - **Call:** do — also lowercase/trim is unnecessary; keep it as proposed. Land before 2.59 (depends on it).
- [x] 2.58 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/provider/openai.rs:166` — retries ignore Retry-After.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `send` sleeps a fixed 1s/2s for 429/5xx and never reads `Retry-After`; a 429 asking for 20s fails after ~3s.
  - **Fix:** in `send`, make the delay a local `let mut wait = Duration::from_secs(1 << (attempt - 1));` and in the `Ok(resp)` branch before `resp.text()` set `if let Some(s) = resp.headers().get(reqwest::header::RETRY_AFTER).and_then(|v| v.to_str().ok()?.parse::<u64>().ok()) { wait = Duration::from_secs(s.min(30)); }` (needs `wait` declared before the `retryable` match). Seconds form only.
  - **Lines:** about -1/+7
  - **Risk:** low — only the sleep length changes; MAX_ATTEMPTS=3 stays, cancellation drops the future cleanly. Edge: `Retry-After: 0` would retry immediately (shorter than the 1s/2s backoff), and a 30 s wait twice is silent in the UI (the turn looks hung up to ~60 s). HTTP-date form and OpenAI's `retry-after-ms` ignored (falls back to old backoff).
  - **Benefit:** medium — 429s with a 5-20 s Retry-After (Anthropic/OpenAI/OpenRouter) stop failing after ~3 s.
  - **Call:** do with care — use `max(backoff, retry_after)` clamped to 30 s so 0 never shortens the wait.
- [x] 2.52 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/provider/openai.rs:39` — HTTP 200 with a plain JSON error body yields "stream ended" and loses the error text.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — a 200 with a bare `{"error":...}` body has no `data:` line, `SseParser` yields nothing, `post_events` returns Ok(false) and the user sees "stream ended before..." (both providers).
  - **Fix:** in `post_events` keep the first ~2 KB of chunks in a `head: Vec<u8>` and a `seen` flag; after the loop, if `parser.finish()` gave nothing and no event was seen and `head` is non-empty, return `ProviderError(format!("unexpected response: {}", String::from_utf8_lossy(&head).trim()))`. One place, covers Chat Completions and Responses.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+9
  - **Risk:** low — only reached when no SSE event was ever seen; the 3 callers (openai.rs, openai_responses.rs, anthropic.rs) share post_events. No code matches the old "stream ended" text. Edge: a stream that ended after only `: keepalive` comments (OpenRouter PROCESSING) would now say "unexpected response: : OPENROUTER PROCESSING" instead of "stream ended before..."; a body that is only whitespace must fall back to the old path.
  - **Benefit:** medium — surfaces the real error text of a 200+JSON error body (common with proxies/OpenRouter) instead of a vague message.
  - **Call:** do with care — skip the new error when the captured head is blank or every non-empty line starts with ':' (comments); otherwise keep old Ok(false).
- [x] 2.88 [checked] `plugins/files/lua/files/pdf.lua:649` — WinAnsi bytes ≥ 0xA0 emitted raw (invalid UTF-8).
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `win_ansi` returns `string.char(code)` for 0xA0..0xFF, a lone high byte (invalid UTF-8); Latin-1 range equals the Unicode code points.
  - **Fix:** Move `win_ansi` below `utf8_char` (LuaJIT has no `utf8` lib) and end it with `return utf8_char(code)` (covers < 0x80 too).
  - **Lines:** about -0/+1
  - **Risk:** low — none found. The only callers are decode_text (two sites), and Latin-1 0xA0-0xFF equals the Unicode code points, so utf8_char gives the right text. win_ansi must be moved below utf8_char (a local function declared later). plugins/files has no pdf tests. bone3/plugins/files is a stale copy (v1.0.0); the live plugin is ~/projects/bone-catalog/plugins/files (3.8.1), whose pdf.lua has the same win_ansi at line 635.
  - **Benefit:** medium — fixes invalid UTF-8 (accented PDF text) reaching the UI and the model.
  - **Call:** do — apply to both pdf.lua copies, and publish the catalog one.
- [x] 1.46 [unverified] `runtime/lua/bone/desktop/tray.lua:97,125` — only 6 rows drawn but selection can move past them with no scroll.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `ui.sel` is clamped to `#rows` but the draw loop is `for i = 1, math.min(#rows, 6)`, so the selection can sit on an undrawn row.
  - **Fix:** draw a window that follows the selection in tray.lua M.node: `local first = math.max(1, ui.sel - 5)` and `for i = first, math.min(#rows, first + 5) do`.
  - **Lines:** about -1/+2
  - **Risk:** low — for sel<=6 first=1 so output is identical; open(r)/selected()/click closures use absolute i and ui.sel, page switch resets sel=1, and height stays 6 rows. Mouse users still cannot reach rows 7+ (no scroll), only keyboard. Lua-only, desktop only (TUI tray is ui/tray.lua, not touched).
  - **Benefit:** medium — selection past row 6 is currently invisible while enter/c act on it (can cancel an unseen task).
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 2.37 [checked] `crates/bone-core/src/mcp/oauth.rs:414` — redirect without `state` accepted.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `code_from` (oauth.rs ~414) uses `q.get("state").is_some_and(|s| *s != self.state)`, so a redirect with no `state` passes the CSRF check.
  - **Fix:** Require it when the pasted text is a URL (a bare pasted code returns earlier): `if q.get("state") != Some(&self.state) { return Err(..same message..) }`. Add "(missing)" to the message only if wanted.
  - **Lines:** about -1/+1
  - **Risk:** low — wait_for_redirect (oauth.rs:366) also routes through code_from, so a /callback hit without state now returns 400 and ends the wait (before: it was accepted); /favicon etc already fail on "no code" and still hit the 404-continue branch. Non-compliant auth servers that drop `state` would break sign-in (OAuth requires echoing it; golden.rs:551 sample includes state). Bare-code pastes unaffected (Url::parse fails -> returned earlier). Existing unit test redirects_are_checked_against_the_state still passes (add nothing).
  - **Benefit:** medium — closes a real CSRF/login-confusion hole on the local redirect listener.
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 2.42 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/mcp/oauth.rs:351` — idle local connection blocks the redirect accept loop.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `wait_for_redirect` does an inline untimed `sock.read` inside the accept loop, so one idle connection (a browser preconnect) blocks the real redirect until the 600s sign-in timeout.
  - **Fix:** Bound that read: `let n = tokio::time::timeout(Duration::from_secs(5), sock.read(&mut buf)).await.ok().and_then(Result::ok).unwrap_or(0);`. An empty request already falls to the existing 404-and-continue branch.
  - **Lines:** about -1/+4
  - **Risk:** low — none found. Empty read (n=0) -> target "/" -> code_from Err and not "/callback" -> existing 404-and-continue branch; a real browser sends its request immediately so 5s is ample (even via SSH port forward). Accept loop stays serial, so an idle preconnect costs at most 5s.
  - **Benefit:** medium — stops a browser preconnect from stalling the whole sign-in until the 600s timeout (real, seen with Chrome).
  - **Call:** do

## P2 — visible annoyances in everyday use

Wrong but survivable: the user sees it and works around it.

- [x] 1.2 [checked] `crates/bone-gui/src/nodeview/paint.rs:672` — controlled input echo: Lua pushing back the text the user just typed counts as a new push (`pushed` only tracks Lua pushes), so the caret jumps to the end on any mid-text edit (desktop sidebar search, `sidebar.lua:612`). Only reset the cursor when the text differs from the buffer.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — paint.rs:672 treats Lua echoing back the typed text as a new push (`pushed` is only the last Lua value), so `cursor = n`.
  - **Fix:** add one helper in nodeview/mod.rs next to `remember_text`: `fn push_text(buf, v: String) { buf.pushed = Some(v.clone()); if buf.text != v { remember_text(buf, v); let n = buf.text.chars().count(); (buf.anchor, buf.cursor) = (n, n); } }`. In `text_input_ui` the push block becomes `if let Some(v) = t.value.as_ref().filter(|v| buf.pushed.as_ref() != Some(*v)) { push_text(buf, v.clone()); }`. An echo equal to the buffer now keeps the caret.
  - **Lines:** about -5/+9 (shared with 1.3 and 1.4)
  - **Risk:** low — only users of `pushed` are paint.rs:672 and ops.rs:201. A Lua value equal to the buffer now keeps the caret; a differing value still moves it to the end, as before. Existing quirk stays: a stale echo that differs from the buffer reverts the text. Catalog files plugin (look.lua:598 search, goto, ask) echoes its value, so it benefits. No test pins caret behavior (tests/node_view.rs has none).
  - **Benefit:** medium — fixes the caret jump in any controlled input that echoes (desktop sidebar search, files plugin).
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 1.3 [checked] `crates/bone-gui/src/nodeview/paint.rs:675` — on a real push, `anchor = anchor.min(n)` while `cursor = n` leaves a stray selection. Set both to n.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `anchor.min(n)` with `cursor = n` leaves a stray selection when the anchor was inside.
  - **Fix:** solved by the same `push_text` helper from 1.2, which sets both anchor and cursor to n (and drops the `anchor.min` line).
  - **Lines:** about -0/+0 (counted in 1.2)
  - **Risk:** low — same push_text helper; anchor and cursor both go to n, so no stray selection. t.select runs after the push block, so select-all on focus is unaffected.
  - **Benefit:** low — removes a stray selection after a Lua push.
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 1.4 [unverified] `crates/bone-gui/src/nodeview/ops.rs:199` — `gui_input_set` replaces text without resetting cursor/anchor. Share one helper with 1.3.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — ops.rs:199 `gui_input_set` sets `pushed` and text but leaves anchor/cursor where they were (Field::new clamps them, so no crash, just a stale caret/selection).
  - **Fix:** replace its three lines (`buf.pushed = ...; remember_text(buf, text);`) with `push_text(buf, text);` using the helper from 1.2.
  - **Lines:** about -2/+1
  - **Risk:** low — gui_input_set (runtime/gui/api.lua:93) is not used by any catalog plugin. When the text differs the caret goes to the end; when it is equal nothing moves.
  - **Benefit:** low — caret follows a programmatic set.
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 1.25 [unverified] `crates/bone-tui/src/app.rs:1066` — opening the same session twice before the load reply creates two ChatBuffers.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `open_session` (app.rs:1063) finds a chat only by `session_id()`, but a chat being loaded has `session == None` until the reply; opening the same id twice pushes a second ChatBuffer. The GUI (chats.rs:105) has the same bug: it sets `loading` but its lookup ignores it.
  - **Fix:** fix it once in bone-ui `find_chat` (chat.rs:1107): `chats.iter().position(|c| c.session_id() == Some(id) || c.loading.as_deref() == Some(id))`. In the TUI also set `self.chats[buf].loading = Some(session_id.clone())` after choosing `buf` (and add `c.loading = None` in its Err arm; `load()` already clears it). That also covers the "untouched" check at app.rs:1075 if `c.loading.is_none()` is added there, as in the GUI.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+4
  - **Risk:** medium — find_chat is shared: TUI chat_by_session, apply_event routing (chat.rs:1120 on! macro, app.rs:1514), Lua data_chat (lua.rs:101) and chat-item `session=` (lua.rs:1454), GUI chats.rs:108. A loading chat (session None) now receives live events for that id (apply_event handlers tolerate session None; chat.rs:338 unwrap is guarded) but load() then wipes them, same as a reload today. TUI: nothing sets/clears `loading` today except load(); needs the Err-arm clear and the untouched check `c.loading.is_none()` (app.rs:1076) or a second open_session(show) would repurpose the loading current chat. A lost reply (server_lost) would leave `loading` set and find_chat would keep matching the stuck chat so reopening shows it empty instead of retrying (GUI already has this).
  - **Benefit:** medium — duplicate chats on double-open in both frontends.
  - **Call:** do with care — add all three TUI pieces (set loading, clear in Err arm, check in untouched test); also consider clearing `loading` on reconnect, and loading check in new_session (app.rs:467).
- [x] 1.29 [unverified] `crates/bone-ui/src/lua/input.rs:369` — `flush_pending` swallows a lone prefix key (e.g. `g` of `gg`) on timeout when the prefix has no action; should replay it.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `flush_pending` (input.rs:369) only replays when a prefix action exists and failed; with `action == None` the pending keys are dropped on timeout, though the mismatch path in `keymap_key` (:309-312) does replay them.
  - **Fix:** unify the two: extract `fn finish_pending(h)` = `let (keys, action, ctx) = take_pending(h); if action.is_none_or(|a| !dispatch_action(h, a)) && h.context() == ctx { replay_literal(h, &ctx, &keys) }`; `flush_pending` calls it (then `prompt_changed()`), and `keymap_key` replaces lines 305-313 with `finish_pending(h)`.
  - **Lines:** about -8/+6
  - **Risk:** low — flush_pending callers (expire_key_sequence, handle_key on context change, paste, focus/clear/delete_context, Host::flush_keys) all pass through the same `h.context() == ctx` guard, so context changes never replay; only prefix-with-no-action now types its literal keys, which type_key already does for non-text keys as a no-op (TUI/GUI type_key). Refactor of keymap_key :305-313 is behaviour-equivalent. Check ordering: flush then prompt_changed() stays after.
  - **Benefit:** medium — a lone `g` of `gg` no longer silently vanishes on timeout/paste/focus change.
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 1.35 [unverified] `runtime/ui/api.lua:1403` — pager counts unwrapped lines for list content, but box wraps them; window too tall, scroll math off.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — for list content `body_lines` returns `content` unwrapped (api.lua:1417), but `bone.ui.box` wraps each line to `inner` (:893, same `w - 4`), so `total`/window height undercount and scroll math is off for lines wider than the box.
  - **Fix:** send lists through the same wrap path as strings: in the `type(content) ~= "string"` branch build `out` by `for _, l in ipairs(content) do for _, r in ipairs(bone.text.wrap(spans(l), width)) do out[#out+1] = r end end` (use `{ {} }` for an empty wrap result, as box does); `spans` is the local at :852.
  - **Lines:** about -1/+8
  - **Risk:** low — box() already wraps list lines at the same `w - 4` width, so the rendered rows are identical; the change only makes total/height/scroll correct. Callers with list content: ui.lua:90, bare.lua:75, tray.lua:52, help.lua, catalog skill/codex/tuis health pagers (lists of span lines/strings) all go through spans(). `spans` local (api.lua:852) is in scope. Empty-wrap case must yield `{ {} }` so blank lines are kept.
  - **Benefit:** medium — long list lines no longer break pager height/scrolling.
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 1.23 [unverified] `crates/bone-tui/src/render.rs:229` — a drag over blank cells copies "" and wipes the system clipboard. Skip when trimmed text is empty.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — render.rs:219-224 copies `s.text(...)` even when every selected cell is blank (`text` trims each row, so a drag over blank cells gives "" or "\n\n"), and `app.clipboard = Some("")` then emits an OSC52 clear. It also fires the `select` event with empty text.
  - **Fix:** make `finished` `Some(...).filter(|t| !t.trim().is_empty())` (render.rs:219-224). `s.copied = true` stays set before the filter, so it does not retry every frame.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+1
  - **Risk:** low — selection only becomes done when non-empty (app.rs:1466), so only all-blank drags are affected; tests.rs:1821-1835 and 4335-4347 select real text and still pass. Filtering means `select` event no longer fires for blank-only selections (desired).
  - **Benefit:** low — stops a blank drag wiping the system clipboard.
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 2.45 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/tools/shell.rs:636,1033` — `write` to a job without stdin is dropped but reports "wrote to <id>".
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `Stdin::Null` spawns with `Stdio::null()` so `input` is None (and `Stdin::Text` / `CloseStdin` set it to None); `Control::Write` then does nothing, yet the tool answers "wrote to <id>".
  - **Fix:** Add `stdin_open: AtomicBool` to `Process`, initialised `matches!(spec.stdin, Stdin::Pipe)`, set false in the `Control::CloseStdin` arm. In `ProcessRegistry::write` and `close_stdin`, `if !p.stdin_open.load(Relaxed) { return Err("job has no open stdin; start it with stdin=\"pipe\"".into()) }`.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+8
  - **Risk:** medium — new error cases: `write` to jobs started without stdin=pipe (Stdin::Null, and Stdin::Text after its text is sent) now errors instead of "wrote to"; `close_stdin` on such a job or a second close_stdin now errors too, which may make models retry/loop after a harmless double close. Setting the flag inside run_job's CloseStdin arm is async, so a write right after close_stdin can still report success. No other callers of ProcessRegistry::write/close_stdin (grep), no catalog/Lua use, tool description (shell.rs:75) already says stdin="pipe" required. PTY (mode=start) jobs use Stdin::Null so write to them also errors (they never accepted writes via Control::Write anyway).
  - **Benefit:** medium — tells the model the truth instead of silently dropping input it believes was delivered.
  - **Call:** do with care — make close_stdin on an already-closed/never-open stdin return Ok ("stdin already closed") and only `write` error; clear the flag synchronously in ProcessRegistry::close_stdin rather than in run_job.
- [x] 2.64 [unverified] `crates/bone-lua/src/wait.rs:28` — `bone.system` timeout doesn't kill grandchildren (no new_group / kill_tree).
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `system()` uses `kill_on_drop` on the shell only and no process group, so a timeout or cancelled wait leaves grandchildren running (and holding the pipes).
  - **Fix:** call `crate::sys::new_group(c.as_std_mut())` before spawn and replace `.kill_on_drop(true)` with a tiny guard holding `child.id()` whose `Drop` calls `crate::sys::kill_tree(pid)` (covers timeout and future-drop cancel; killing an already-exited group is harmless). Same helpers shell.rs and mcp.rs use.
  - **Lines:** about -1/+10
  - **Risk:** high — as proposed the guard's Drop calls kill_tree(pid) on EVERY exit incl. normal completion. `bone.system("xdg-open url")` (catalog mcp/ui.lua:186-190, bone3 plugins/files/core.lua:16 files.open) and any `cmd &` helper leave a browser/viewer in the shell's process group; SIGKILL of -pid on completion would kill it. (ssh plugin uses `setsid -f`, unaffected.) Also pid reuse after the child is reaped makes an unconditional kill_tree unsafe. new_group also moves children out of the foreground tty group (SIGINT no longer reaches them; /dev/tty readers get SIGTTIN) — acceptable but notable.
  - **Benefit:** medium — timeout/cancel currently leaves grandchildren running and holding pipes.
  - **Call:** revise — keep kill_on_drop, call new_group, and kill the tree ONLY on the timeout branch and on future-drop before completion (arm the guard, disarm it once wait_with_output returns Ok).
- [x] 2.77 [unverified] `crates/bone-ui/src/lua.rs:988` — `fire()` snapshot: a handler that `off`s a later one causes a spurious error.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — fire() snapshots callback ids; if an earlier handler calls `off` on a later one, `call_callback` does `registry.get::<Function>(id)` on a nil and reports a conversion error.
  - **Fix:** snapshot `(a.id, a.callback)` and before each call skip if `!h.autocmds().iter().any(|a| a.id == id)`. Three lines in fire() in bone-ui/src/lua.rs.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+4
  - **Risk:** low — none found. fire() is the only dispatcher (tui `App::fire` delegates to it; no GUI copy). The extra `autocmds().iter().any(|a| a.id == id)` check per handler is O(n^2) in handlers per event, which is negligible. Snapshot `(a.id, a.callback)` and check by the autocmd id.
  - **Benefit:** medium — removes a real spurious error whenever a handler offs a later handler or a plugin unloads mid-event.
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 6.1 [checked] `crates/bone-core/src/runtime.rs:156` → `provider::built_in` — new `reqwest::Client` per call for sessions pinned to a non-default model (fresh TLS per agent step). Same in `bone-lua/src/wait.rs:72` (`bone.http`) and `health.rs:119`. One shared client.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `agent.rs:430` runs `rt.pinned()` on every model call (each agent step, compaction, `bone.model`); a pin that differs from the default goes through `provider_for` -> `built_in` -> `http_client()`, which builds a new `reqwest::Client` (new pool, new TLS handshake, no keep-alive) each time. `bone.http` (`wait.rs:72`) builds `Client::new()` per Lua call, which also reloads TLS roots. `health.rs:119` is a one-off health check (cold).
  - **Fix:** In `provider/openai.rs` make `http_client()` return a clone of a `static CLIENT: OnceLock<reqwest::Client>` (a Client is an Arc, so the clone is free). Do the same in `bone-lua/src/wait.rs::send` with its own OnceLock (a separate crate and it has no connect timeout, so it can't share). Point `health.rs` at `crate::provider::http_client()`, since the per-request `.timeout(5s)` still applies. Caveat: tests that spin up several tokio runtimes may reuse idle connections owned by a dead runtime. Check the test suite once.
  - **Lines:** about -2/+8
  - **Risk:** low — http_client() takes no config (only a 30 s connect_timeout; per-provider base_url/api_key/headers/model are applied per request in provider/*.rs, timeouts per request in health.rs/wait.rs), so a static client loses no per-provider setting. Checked all builders: openai.rs:101 (also feeds provider/mod.rs:124 built_in, scripting.rs:676 LuaProvider::new which is ALSO rebuilt per step via runtime.rs:195 and gets fixed for free), wait.rs:72, health.rs:120; mcp.rs:573 and oauth.rs:269/462 are separate and untouched. reqwest has no http2 feature here (HTTP/1 pool only). Residual: (a) clients are used from several runtimes (main rt, Lua thread's own 2-worker rt at scripting.rs:979, bone-ui h.spawn -> wait::run); a pooled conn driven by a dropped runtime (Scripting rebuild, per-test #[tokio::test] rt) is evicted by hyper and idempotent-retried, so only a rare "connection closed" on the first request after a rebuild; (b) system proxy env is frozen at first use (nothing in repo sets proxy env); (c) pinned sessions to a restarted local server now share the stale-keepalive race the default provider already has (send() retries connect/timeout/429/5xx only). The proposed health.rs change adds a needless 30 s connect_timeout/pool reuse to a cold probe whose point is a fresh connect.
  - **Benefit:** medium — pinned sessions (any pin != default, runtime.rs:97) currently pay a fresh TCP+TLS handshake and webpki root parse every agent step/compaction; bone.http likewise per call.
  - **Call:** do with care — apply to openai.rs http_client() and wait.rs only (separate OnceLock, plain Client::new() semantics, no connect timeout); leave health.rs as is; run bone-core provider/scripting tests (bone.http in scripting/tests.rs:388-433, anthropic/tests.rs:322) a few times for runtime-reuse flakiness.
- [x] 6.4 [checked] `crates/bone-core/src/provider/openai.rs:180` — `encode(body)` inside the retry loop (and serializes twice). `json!({messages: vec})` deep-copies the transcript in all three providers.
  - **Verdict:** partly — `json!({ "messages": wire_messages(..) })` really deep-copies the whole wire transcript once per model call in all three providers (`openai.rs:234`, `openai_responses.rs:45`, `anthropic.rs:138`): `json!` does `to_value(&expr)` on a Value, which clones it, base64 images included. The `encode()` inside the retry loop only re-runs after a failed attempt followed by a sleep of at least 1 s, so it is negligible; hoisting it is optional. The two-pass serialize is deliberate (documented alloc-once trick); leave it.
  - **Fix:** Stop passing the Vec through `json!`. Keep the key order (the repo sorts keys for prompt-cache stability): write `"messages": Value::Null` in the `json!` as a placeholder, then `body["messages"] = Value::Array(wire_messages(..))`. Assigning an existing key keeps its position and moves the Vec. Same for `input` and the Anthropic `messages`. Optional: hoist `let payload = Bytes::from(encode(body))` above the retry loop and pass `payload.clone()`.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+4
  - **Risk:** low — Anthropic: `messages` is not used after the json! (fingerprint/breakpoints run before), so moving it is safe; openai/responses wire_* results are only used in the json!. serde_json here is BTreeMap-ordered (no preserve_order feature in Cargo.toml), and even with IndexMap, assigning an existing key keeps its slot, so prompt-cache key order/fingerprint cannot change; configured()/merge overlays operate on the final Value unchanged. Placeholder must be written as "messages"/"input" key and overwritten, not appended after (tests comparing body JSON would still pass).
  - **Benefit:** medium — saves one whole-transcript deep copy (base64 images included) per model call in all three providers and the transient 2x memory peak.
  - **Call:** do — skip the optional encode() hoist (retry-only, and Bytes clone vs Vec changes the send() signature).
- [x] 6.16 [unverified] Lua per-frame: `help.lua:132` reparses all docs per list() [checked]; `settings_page.lua:31` option list rebuilt per render; `menu.lua:242` command list per prompt change; `catalog.lua:110,452` SHA-256 of every plugin file per index, rows re-sorted per call; `desktop/tools.lua:500` LCS diff per render (even collapsed); `desktop/help.lua:24`, `pick.lua:57` markdown reparse per rebuild; `sidebar.lua:186` sorts every chat per render; `ui/views.lua:366` re-wraps whole live shell buffer per chunk; `ui/views.lua:61` wipes stream caches per draw; `dot_matrix.lua:44` settings copy at 20 fps; `ui/api.lua:1102` `chat.count` copies items; `ui/api.lua:1218,1435` select/pager recompute per frame; `files/gui.lua:669` markdown reparse per keystroke; `files/core.lua:24` PDF reparsed each selection (no cache); `pdf.lua` per-byte inflate/tokenizer, font_info per page.

  - **Fix:**
  - `help.lua:132` `M.list`: confirmed, `doc_sections()` reparses every embedded doc (thousands of lines) on every call, even for tabs 1 and 2 that never use `docs`. (done: a and c; b, the streamed cache, left out) Move the `docs`/`sections` construction into the `else` (tab 3) branch. That removes the work for two of three tabs and costs no lines. Tab 3 keeps its per-keystroke parse and is fine as is, because the browser is a dialog. Don't add a cache.
  - `ui/views.lua:61` `streamed`: confirmed bug-shaped. Every non-streaming item draw does `kept = {}`, wiping the settled-prefix cache of the streaming item, so the "settled part drawn once" optimisation is lost whenever any other item redraws. Replace with `kept[item.kind .. item.key] = nil`.
  - `ui/api.lua:1435` pager: `body_lines(inner)` wraps the whole text (a /help doc is ~1000+ lines, one `bone.text.wrap` call per line) on every draw, so every scroll key or wheel tick re-wraps it. Memoise the result by `inner` width and reset the memo in `handle:set`. About +5 lines, no invalidation beyond width and `set`.
  - `ui/views.lua:366` `gutter` on live shell output: bounded by LIVE_MAX (64 KiB) and by the frame cap, so a few ms per redraw of a noisy command. Folding it would change the "hidden rows" count. Leave.
  - `settings_page.lua:31` option list: ~100 `pcall(bone.o.info)` plus a sort per render of an open settings dialog. Leave.
  - `menu.lua:242`: `M.all` returns immediately unless the prompt starts with "/"; then it is ~100 commands, filtered and sorted. Leave.
  - `catalog.lua:110,452`: SHA-256 per installed file once per `M.index` (opening the catalog or after an install), and a ~30-row sort per render. Leave.
  - `desktop/tools.lua:500` diff: runs when an item is (re)drawn, on small edit strings. Leave.
  - `desktop/help.lua:24`, `pick.lua:57` markdown reparse: per rebuild of a dialog. Leave.
  - `sidebar.lua:186` sort: per rebuild over tens to hundreds of chats. Leave.
  - `dot_matrix.lua:44`: empty-chat TUI only, at 20 fps, one small settings read. Leave.
  - `ui/api.lua:1102` `chat.count`: no caller in `runtime/` or `plugins/`. Leave.
  - `ui/api.lua:1218` select: filters a short list per frame. Leave.
  - `files/gui.lua:669`: already cached on `md_text ~= body`. A changed buffer must reparse, so rejected.
  - `files/core.lua:24` / `pdf.lua`: bounded (16 MB, 20 pages) and niche, though it blocks core Lua for a large PDF. A real fix is a Rust inflater (new dep) or an mtime-keyed cache. Leave.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+6
  - **Risk:** medium — three Lua sub-changes of differing safety. (a) help.lua M.list: moving docs/sections into tab-3 branch is safe: `docs`/`sections` are used only for `all = docs`; doc_sections() is pure; `guide` map and keys stay outside; choose() calls doc_sections() fresh and `section = i` indexes are produced only by tab 3, so consistent. Callers: help.lua:206 (TUI) and desktop/help.lua:64. (b) views.lua:61 streamed: replacing `kept = {}` with `kept[id] = nil` removes the only eviction of `kept`; entries for a streaming item that never gets a non-streaming redraw (chat closed/cleared or aborted mid-stream, item key reused) now live forever holding the full streamed text and lines (unbounded leak across a long session). Also cached settled prefix now survives mid-stream config/markdown-option changes (key checks only width and text prefix; styles are group names so theme change is OK). Also id collision across chats (kind..key) was already possible. (c) api.lua:1435 pager memo: only valid for string content. List-of-lines content (returned as-is) and element content (elements.lines) can be mutated in place by callers (e.g. ui.lua:90/bare.lua:75 build lists then update via bone.ui.update) and would go stale; handle:set resets `content` so it must clear the memo. Pager wrap path is TUI-only (GUI sets windows.pager = desktop/pick.lua:50, which does not use render), box() (api.lua:862) does not mutate span rows so sharing memoised rows is safe.
  - **Benefit:** medium — help tab 1/2 no longer reparse all docs per keystroke; pager scroll stops rewrapping 1000+ lines per key; streamed prefix cache survives other-item redraws (only a gain when items redraw mid-stream).
  - **Call:** revise — do (a) as proposed; do (c) but memoise only when type(content)=="string", key = inner width, clear memo in handle:set; for (b) use `kept[id]=nil` only together with a bound (e.g. also drop entries whose item is no longer streaming at next non-streaming draw of same id, or clear `kept` when it holds more than a few entries / on chat switch) so abandoned streams cannot leak.

Behavior change: one bad saved field no longer discards all saved compact fields (what the doc says); a typo is now silent (stderr line removed; that eprintln also scribbled on the TUI screen every model call). Cost: a from_value per saved field per call, negligible. Check CompactConfig for deny_unknown_fields (then unknown keys are skipped per field, fine).

  - **Call:** do — keep the final limit/provider/prompt filters; no tests needed.

Cells: in `split_row` use `t.replace("\\|", "\u{1}").split('|').map(|c| c.trim().replace('\u{1}', "|"))`.

Table fix: `split_row`'s `strip_suffix('|')` runs before the replace, so a row ending in `\|` still loses that pipe (leaves a stray `\`); do the sentinel replace before the prefix/suffix stripping. Also feeds is_header_separator/table_align (harmless).
  - **Benefit:** low — ~~~ inside ``` blocks and `\|` in tables render right.
  - **Call:** do with care — apply the `\|` replace before stripping leading/trailing pipes.
- [x] 1.32 [unverified] `crates/bone-ui/src/text.rs:174` — `clip_groups` drops spans with no "…" when a span exactly fills the width.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — in `clip_groups`, when a span exactly fills the remaining room (`used + w == width`) and more spans follow, the `else` calls `truncate(&t, width - used)`, which returns `t` unchanged (no "…") and stops, so the cut shows no ellipsis.
  - **Fix:** in the else branch use `truncate(&t, (width - used).min(w - 1))` (w >= 1 there, and w >= width - used), which forces a cut and so the "…" whenever there is more to drop.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+0 (one expression)

Only caller is state.rs:136 (bone.text clip) plus the text.rs:306 test (unchanged: 3<4). Behaviour change: exact-fit spans now end in "…" (width-used==w==1 gives a bare "…").

  - **Risk:** low — none found. Timeout drop fires CancelOnDrop (notifications/cancelled) for LineConn; HTTP path just drops the request. On timeout the old tool list stays (stale but safe). Reusing START_TIMEOUT 30s is the same budget as initial list.

- [x] 2.35 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/mcp.rs:416` — HTTP path never answers server pings.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `HttpConn::request` passes `|_| {}` as the reply closure to `handle_incoming`, so a server `ping` (or any request) inside an SSE response is never answered.
  - **Fix:** Replace `|_| {}` with a closure that posts the reply: `|r| { let rb = self.post(&r); tokio::spawn(async move { let _ = rb.send().await; }); }` (RequestBuilder is owned and Send; session/auth headers are already applied by `post`). Unsupported methods then also get their -32601 reply.
  - **Lines:** about -1/+4
  - **Risk:** low — none found. handle_incoming reply closure is Fn(Value); `self.post(&r)` builds an owned RequestBuilder (adds auth/session headers), spawned detached; compiles in async ctx. Test server in mcp/tests.rs (SSE only sends a notification, unknown POST bodies get 202) is unaffected; the extra POST only happens if a server sends a request in the stream. Detached send has no timeout (reqwest Client::new) so a hung server leaves a leaked task; negligible. Unsupported methods now get -32601 over HTTP, spec-correct.
  - **Benefit:** low — servers that ping/probe inside SSE streams currently get no reply (rare).
  - **Call:** do
- [x] 2.89 [checked] `plugins/files/lua/files/pdf.lua:852` — `]`, `{`, `}` advance twice, skipping a byte.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — the `]`/`{`/`}` branch and the "nothing matched" else both do `pos = pos + 1` with `token == nil`, then `if not token then pos = next_pos or (pos + 1)` advances again, skipping a byte.

  - **Risk:** low — none found. Both edits are needed: delete the `]`/`{`/`}` branch and also the `pos = pos + 1` in the innermost else; otherwise the byte is still skipped twice. Check that the else removal leaves valid `if word then ... end` syntax.

- [x] 2.90 [unverified] `pdf.lua:490` (adds keys during pairs), `762,886` (unknown font returns raw bytes), `1020` (whole file read before size check), `1030` (pages past 20 dropped silently), `806` (inline images not skipped), `980` (no visited set), `573` (unsupported filters/encryption not reported). **Done in part (bone-catalog):** ObjStm iteration, missing font, size-limited read, page-tree cycles. Not done: inline-image skip, page-cap note, encryption message.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — read_object_streams assigns `doc.objects[num]` while iterating `pairs(doc.objects)` (undefined in Lua); decode_text returns raw bytes for a missing font; `file:read("*a")` precedes the size check; pages past MAX_PAGES are silently dropped; no `BI/ID/EI` handling so inline-image bytes get tokenized as content; find_pages' `walk` has depth 32 but no visited set (a Kids list repeating a node fans out exponentially); unsupported filters and /Encrypt end as "may be a scan".
  - **Fix:** read_object_streams: first collect the ObjStm objects into a list, then loop over it. decode_text: `font = font or NO_FONT` with `NO_FONT = { unicode = {}, diffs = {} }`. M.text: `file:read(M.MAX_BYTES + 1)` (size check then works unchanged). Page cap: after the loop add `if #pages > M.MAX_PAGES then text = text .. "\n\n(Only the first N of M pages are shown.)"`. Inline images: on op `ID` do `local _, e = content:find("%sEI%s", next_pos); pos = e or #content + 1`. walk: `seen[node]` table, return if seen. Encrypt: `if data:sub(-2048):find("/Encrypt", 1, true) then return nil, "this PDF is encrypted" end` (the "unsupported filter" report can share that message path via a `doc.unsupported` flag, skip unless wanted).
  - **Lines:** about -3/+16
  - **Risk:** low-medium — seven independent edits with no tests. Notes: (a) `font = font or NO_FONT` makes a missing font decode through win_ansi instead of returning raw bytes, which is fine but should land with 2.88. (b) `file:read(M.MAX_BYTES + 1)` returns nil for an empty file, so the message becomes "could not be read" instead of "not a PDF"; harmless. (c) The ID/EI skip with `%sEI%s` fails when EI is the last bytes of the stream, so use a pattern that also matches end of stream, and fall back to continuing if no EI is found rather than dropping the rest. (d) Detecting /Encrypt by scanning the last 2048 bytes can miss an xref-stream trailer and false-positive on stray text; use it only to pick the error message when no text was extracted. (e) The object-stream collection loop drops the incidental discovery of nested ObjStm, which the spec forbids. (f) The page-cap note has to go in before the MAX_TEXT truncation.
  - **Benefit:** medium — fixes undefined-behavior iteration (pairs plus insert), a possible exponential walk, an unbounded read, and silently dropped pages.
  - **Call:** do with care — land as separate small commits and run a few real PDFs (an ObjStm/xref-stream one, one with inline images, an encrypted one). Apply to both pdf.lua copies.

- [x] 2.83 [unverified] `crates/bone-tui/src/clipboard.rs:116` — stderr reader stops at 4096 bytes; helper can deadlock.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — the stderr thread reads at most 4096 bytes and exits; a helper that writes more than the pipe buffer to stderr blocks, never closes stdout, and `read_to_end` on stdout hangs.
  - **Fix:** In the thread, after the capped read, `let _ = std::io::copy(&mut stderr, &mut std::io::sink());`. Use `read_to_end` into a Vec and `from_utf8_lossy` so non-UTF-8 stderr doesn't discard the message.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+3
  - **Risk:** low — none found. The grandchild-holding-stderr hang already exists today (the old read blocked until EOF or 4096 bytes), so draining does not make it worse. Reading bytes and using from_utf8_lossy fixes read_to_string discarding everything when stderr holds non-UTF-8. `Read` is already in scope for `.take`.
  - **Benefit:** low — avoids a clipboard helper hang when a tool is noisy on stderr; the helper is killed by the 10s timeout anyway.
  - **Call:** do
- [ ] 2.69 [unverified] `crates/bone-proto/src/client.rs:200` — Windows `open_pipe` loops forever on PIPE_BUSY.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `open_pipe` loops forever on ERROR_PIPE_BUSY every 50 ms; a wedged server hangs `bone` at startup with no message.
  - **Fix:** bound it: `for _ in 0..100 { match ... }` (5 s) and then `Err(io::ErrorKind::TimedOut.into())`, or wrap the call in `connect` with `tokio::time::timeout(Duration::from_secs(5), open_pipe(..))` and map to TimedOut.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+4
  - **Risk:** low — Windows-only; open_pipe is used by connect() for attach, spawn_core's poll loop (treats errors as retryable until its 10 s deadline) and reconnect() (own 5 s timeout), so a TimedOut error after ~5 s is handled by all three. Cannot be tested on Linux.
  - **Benefit:** low — avoids an unbounded hang at startup against a wedged pipe server.
  - **Call:** do — prefer the 100-iteration bound inside open_pipe (keeps cancellation semantics) and return ErrorKind::TimedOut.
- [ ] 3.17 [unverified] `runtime/tui/bare.lua` — bare can't reach paste_clipboard, word delete, focus_next, new_session, reasoning toggle, tool_detail, tray. Add bindings or document the subset. Also duplicates `ui.lua` sessions/help/health (31-81).

Say in the header that reasoning/tool_detail/tray keys belong to standard views that bare does not draw. To remove the duplication, hoist the bodies of sessions/help/health out of `M.setup` in lua/bone/ui.lua into `M.sessions`, `M.help`, `M.health` (they only use globals). Setup assigns them as `bone.ui.sessions = M.sessions` and so on. bare.lua does `local std = require("bone.ui")` and assigns the same three, and its own copies (lines 50-81) are deleted.

In bare tab-in-main would shadow a future completion and do nothing useful without a panel. Use ctrl+p (main) and tab with { context = "panel" }, mirroring keys.lua:41/89. (b) ctrl+w/ctrl+left etc. names match keymap.rs (DeleteWord, WordLeft, WordRight, PasteClipboard, NewSession) so they are valid. (c) Dedup: sessions and help bodies are identical, hoisting them is safe (require("bone.ui") returns lua/bone/ui.lua, embedded at lib.rs:41; bare libraries made with create_tui_library("bare") get only defaults.lua and fall back to embedded modules, so require works). But health is NOT a duplicate: bare's is deliberately plain (Normal-only, ascii marks, no colorscheme groups); hoisting standard health into bare would change /health in bare to use Accent/DiffAdd/ErrorMsg groups that bare doesn't define. Also hoisting changes `M.setup` closures into module functions that must not capture setup-local state (they only use globals — ok).
  - **Benefit:** low-medium — bare gains paste/word-edit/new-session keys (real usability gap); dedup saves ~12 lines.
  - **Call:** revise — add the keys but with ctrl+p (main) and tab in "panel" context, say in the header that reasoning/tool_detail/tray keys are not drawn in bare; hoist only sessions and help into bone.ui (M.sessions/M.help), keep bare's plain health.

- [ ] 1.38 [unverified] `runtime/lua/bone/nodes/widgets.lua:309` — `content:sub(1, fit)` cuts UTF-8 by bytes.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `content:sub(1, fit)` (widgets.lua:309) is a byte cut and `#content > fit` compares bytes, so multibyte text can be split mid-character (invalid UTF-8) and cut early.
  - **Fix:** `local n = utf8.len(content) or #content; if n > fit then content = content:sub(1, (utf8.offset(content, fit + 1) or #content + 1) - 1):gsub("%s+%S*$", "") .. "…" end` (replaces the `#content` test and the `sub`).

  - **Risk:** high — the runtime is LuaJIT (Cargo.toml mlua features "luajit"; terminal.lua:498 notes "LuaJIT has no utf8 library"), so `utf8.len`/`utf8.offset` are nil and the proposed code throws "attempt to index global 'utf8'" whenever a detail text is drawn (every dialog using M.detail with a string) in the GUI.
  - **Benefit:** low — avoids cutting a multibyte char in a two-line hint.
  - **Call:** revise — no utf8: cut by bytes then drop a trailing partial sequence, e.g. `local cut = content:sub(1, fit):gsub("[\192-\255][\128-\191]*$", ""):gsub("%s+%S*$", "")` (careful: gsub returns 2 values) or use bone.text.truncate(content, fit) after measuring with bone.text.width.
