Scheme-less base_urls are non-local both before and after. 127.0.0.0/8 stays local via is_loopback; LAN remains remote as proposed.
  - **Benefit:** low — correct advisory warning.
  - **Call:** do with care — implement with host_str + IpAddr parse; no test pins "none (local server)".
- [ ] 6.15 [unverified] TUI per-frame: layout uncached, prompt spec computed twice (`render.rs:295,398`); `natural_height` wraps to count (`prompt.rs:99`); `term.rs:215` `format!` + theme lookup per cell; `data.rs:174` turns O(turns × items); `lua.rs:1422` `chat_items` rebuilds Indexed per call; popup/panel clones per frame; key events allocate with no handlers (`input.rs:191,384`); job handle table per chunk (`lua/jobs.rs:325`).
  - **Verdict:** partly — TUI redraws are dirty-driven and frame-capped, and the per-frame items are tiny (a Lua prompt-spec call, one short wrap). Two sub-points are worth a cheap change.
  - **Fix:**
  - `term.rs:215` `group_of` per cell: confirmed. Each cell with colour does ~6 allocations (`format!` x2, the code strings, the flag String, the name) plus a theme lookup, so a 50x200 terminal view is ~10k cells per redraw, on every output chunk while a /term or job view is visible. Track the previous cell's `(fgcolor, bgcolor, bold, italic, underline, inverse)` and call `group_of` only when it changes; otherwise reuse the run's current group. Cuts the allocations to roughly one per run.
  - `data.rs:174` `turns()`: O(turns x items) via `items.iter().enumerate().filter(turns[n] == t)` per turn. Only the public `bone.chat.turns()` calls it and nothing in `runtime/` does, so it is cold. Since `turns[]` is monotonic, slice each turn with `partition_point` instead of filtering. Optional and tiny.
  - Layout uncached, prompt spec computed twice (`render.rs:295,398`): a Lua callback call, microseconds. Plumbing the spec from layout to draw is more code than it saves. Leave.
  - `natural_height` wraps the prompt to count rows (prompt.rs:99): the draft is short. Leave.
  - `lua.rs:1422` `chat_items` rebuilding `Indexed`: an O(items) `turns_of` per call. Its callers are per-key or per-item-redraw (`tui/defaults.lua:54`, views), so about 10us at 4000 items. Leave.
  - Popup/panel clones, key events with no handlers (`input.rs:191,384`: one `json!` per human keypress), job handle per chunk (`lua/jobs.rs:325`: one Lua call per output chunk): negligible. Leave.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+10
  - **Risk:** low — term.rs sub-point: tracking the previous non-continuation cell's (fgcolor,bgcolor,bold,italic,underline,inverse) and reusing the run's group is exactly equivalent, since group_of is a pure function of those six fields (plus theme.has/set_linked side effect that already ran for that group earlier in the same row). Must reset the tracker per row and only update it for cells that are not wide-continuations (those `continue` before group_of today), and the first cell of each row must call group_of. No theme-reload staleness since nothing persists across lines() calls. The data.rs `turns()` change is behavior-neutral if done right (turns[] monotonic; members[0] must stay the user item) but is cold: only bone.chat.turns() (runtime/ui/api.lua:1108, tests) calls it.
  - **Benefit:** low — term.rs saves ~10k tiny String allocs per redraw of a visible /term view (~sub-ms); data.rs gain is nil.
  - **Call:** do with care — implement term.rs only (keep Normal-vs-@term comparison semantic via the group string, run tests plays_output_into_a_screen_with_colors in term.rs); skip the data.rs partition_point change (cold, nonzero rewrite risk for no gain).

## P3 — rare edge cases and hardening

Needs disk failure, races, misuse by a plugin author or an unusual setup to show up.

- [ ] 1.1 [checked] `crates/bone-core/src/runtime.rs:650` — `queue_remove` with no `queue_id` falls into `_ => s.queue.clear()`, so `bone.queue.remove(id)` with a nil id wipes the queue. An unknown id is accepted silently (`QueueRemove` in lib.rs:706 errors instead). Split the arms; require queue_id.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — the `_ =>` arm in runtime.rs:650-654 clears the queue for `queue_remove` with a nil id.
  - **Fix:** keep the shared arm but make the id mandatory for remove, and fold the `if op == "queue_clear"` check into the match: `let qid = (op == "queue_remove").then(|| spec["queue_id"].as_u64().ok_or("bone.queue.remove needs a queue id")).transpose()?;` then `match qid { Some(q) => s.queue.retain(|x| x.id != q), None => { s.queue.clear(); s.queue_paused = false } }`. An unknown id staying a silent no-op is fine (idempotent); matching lib.rs's `no_item` error is optional.
  - **Lines:** about -4/+4
  - **Risk:** low — none found. Only caller is runtime/core/api.lua:261 (bone.queue.remove); no repo or bone-catalog Lua calls it with a nil id. Lua->JSON turns integral numbers into ints (bone-lua lib.rs:749), so as_u64 works. lib.rs QueueRemove is a separate path.
  - **Benefit:** low — a nil id no longer wipes the queue.
  - **Call:** do
- [ ] 1.18 [checked] `crates/bone-ui/src/lua.rs:1010` — `fire_submit`: every handler gets the original text and the last string wins, so rewrites don't chain.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `fire` calls all handlers with the same data, so `fire_submit` can't chain rewrites.
  - **Fix:** extract the callback list of `fire` into a small `fn callbacks<H: Host>(h: &mut H, event: &str) -> Vec<u64>` used by both; `fire_submit` then loops `for cb in callbacks(h, "submit") { let data = json!({ "text": text, "images": images }); match h.call_callback(cb, "autocmd submit", |lua| to_lua(lua, &data)) { Some(Value::Boolean(false)) => return None, Some(Value::String(s)) => text = s.to_string_lossy(), _ => {} } }`, building `data` per handler from the current text.
  - **Lines:** about -4/+10
  - **Risk:** medium — fire_submit currently runs every handler even when one returns false. background.lua:266 (queue-edit save) returns false and :288 (clear finished rows) is registered after it, and catalog todo (ui.lua:68) listens on "submit". Returning None at the first false would skip the later observers on a canceled submit (arguably more correct, but a behavior change). Handlers now see the rewritten text, which is the goal. Both TUI (app.rs:976) and GUI (lib.rs:432) use it.
  - **Benefit:** low — only matters for chained rewriting handlers.
  - **Call:** do with care — decide whether remaining handlers run after a cancel (safest: record cancelled = true, keep calling the rest with the current text, return None at the end); test with two rewriting handlers and one canceling handler.
- [ ] 2.2 [checked] `crates/bone-core/src/session.rs:325` — `compact()` mutates memory before the checkpoint write result is known.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `Session::compact` replaces messages/summary/moves/generation before looking at `written`; the caller then reports "cannot save session" while memory already changed.
  - **Fix:** Write first and bail: `write_record(&mut self.file, Record::Compact(messages.clone())).and_then(|()| self.file.sync_data())?;` then mutate. Same reorder in `summarize` (set `self.summary` only after the `?`).
  - **Lines:** about -4/+3
  - **Risk:** low — Session::compact has callers runtime.rs:719 (bone.session.compact) and agent_tests; all already map the io::Error to "cannot save session". Other writers (cd) already write-then-mutate, so this matches. Residual: write ok + sync_data fails leaves the file ahead of memory (before: memory ahead of file); a partial write_all still leaves a torn tail (unchanged). session.rs tests at ~1055 still pass (they unwrap Ok). summarize callers compact.rs:698/728 same.
  - **Benefit:** low — only matters on disk-full/IO errors.
  - **Call:** do — also reorder summarize; no extra changes needed.
- [ ] 2.22 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/agent.rs:414` — `let _ = pin(...)` ignores the write error; pinned in memory, not on disk.
  - **Verdict:** partly — `Session::pin` sets `self.model` before writing, and `drive` ignores the error, so memory and disk can disagree; losing a pin on a failing disk is minor like a lost usage record.
  - **Fix:** Reorder `pin` to write first: `write_record(&mut self.file, Record::Model(model.clone()))?; self.model = Some(model); Ok(())`. A failed write then leaves the session unpinned and the next call retries it; `let _ =` in agent.rs can stay.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+1
  - **Risk:** low — Session::pin callers: agent.rs:414 (`let _ =`), lib.rs:558/563 (session start/model set, use `?`, so now a failed write reports an error without changing memory, which is better), agent_tests. After a failed write the session stays unpinned, so the next model call in that turn re-pins (agent.rs only pins when `model` is None); until then the session follows the runtime default, as it did before the first pin. No old-file format change.
  - **Benefit:** low — disk-failure edge only.
  - **Call:** do
- [ ] 2.6 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/lib.rs:1102` — `session_delete` checks `active` then deletes without holding the lock; a turn starting in between writes to a deleted file.
  - **Verdict:** partly — the check-then-delete gap is real (`session_delete` drops the lock before `sessions.delete`), but the harm is a turn writing to an unlinked file, narrow and self-limiting.
  - **Fix:** Hold the guard across the delete: `let s = session.lock().unwrap(); if let Some(active) = &s.active { return Err(BUSY) } ... sessions.delete(...)?; drop(s);` (delete()/cancel_owner don't lock the session). That shrinks the race to a turn that already holds the handle; closing it fully needs a `deleted` flag, not worth it.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+2
  - **Risk:** low — holding the session guard across sessions.delete is deadlock-free: delete() takes `loaded`/`listed`/index locks, and the only code holding `loaded` (keep()) uses try_lock on sessions; list() clones `loaded` and releases before locking sessions; jobs.cancel_owner locks only process maps. Session::reindex already calls index.sync under the session lock, so session->index order is established. Must keep the guard alive past `cancel_owner` and drop it before emit/started.lock.
  - **Benefit:** low — narrows a narrow race; full close needs a `deleted` flag.
  - **Call:** do with care — hold the guard only through delete, drop before `started.lock()`/emit.
- [ ] 2.12 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/index.rs:136` — version migration in a deferred transaction; two processes can drop each other's tables. Use BEGIN IMMEDIATE.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed (low severity, the index is derived and refills) — version is read before `BEGIN` (deferred), so two processes can both migrate and the second drops the first's filled tables.
  - **Fix:** Do the migration inside `let tx = conn.transaction_with_behavior(TransactionBehavior::Immediate)?` and read `PRAGMA user_version` inside it (make `conn` `mut`); `execute_batch(drop+SCHEMA+pragma)` on `tx`, `commit()`. Drops the hand-written `BEGIN;`/`COMMIT;` strings.
  - **Lines:** about -3/+4
  - **Risk:** low — Index::open_once is the sole place; `conn` must become `mut`, and `PRAGMA user_version = N` inside the Immediate transaction is fine (current code already does it inside BEGIN). busy_timeout is set before, so a second process waits instead of failing; version must be re-read inside the tx (proposal does). SCHEMA (incl. any virtual/FTS tables) already ran inside BEGIN.
  - **Benefit:** low — two simultaneous version bumps only; index refills.
  - **Call:** do
- [ ] 2.11 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/index.rs:164` — failed reopen during corrupt-index recovery leaves the in-memory placeholder; syncs go nowhere until restart.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — in `sync`, `*conn = Connection::open_in_memory()` then `Self::open_once(&self.path)?` returns early on failure, leaving an empty in-memory db; `corrupt()` on it is false so it never retries.
  - **Fix:** Make the recovery condition also fire for the placeholder: `corrupt(&conn) || conn.path().is_none_or(|p| p.as_os_str().is_empty())` (rusqlite `Connection::path`), so the next failing sync (after the 1/min gate) quarantines nothing and reopens. Alternative: drop `checked` rate limit on open failure.
  - **Lines:** about -1/+2
  - **Risk:** medium — as written, treating the in-memory placeholder (rusqlite 0.34 `Connection::path()` returns Some("") for in-memory) as "corrupt" makes sync() call quarantine(&self.path), which renames whatever now sits at the real path to .db.corrupt, including a healthy DB another core process opened/created meanwhile, and then open_once again. Also `is_none_or` needs Rust 1.82+.
  - **Benefit:** low — index is derived; only matters after a double failure (corrupt + reopen fails), searches stay stale until restart.
  - **Call:** revise — add a separate branch: if the conn is the placeholder (path empty), just retry `Self::open_once(&self.path)` (no quarantine) under the same 1/min gate; leave the corrupt branch alone.
- [ ] 2.43 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/mcp/oauth.rs:492,67` — refresh mutex held across untimed request; store() not under it (lost entries). `oauth.rs:77` tmp file keeps old perms, no fsync.
  - **Verdict:** partly — the untimed request under the refresh mutex is fixed by the 2.39 client timeout. `store()`/`sign_out()` are an unlocked load-modify-save, so a refresh and an exchange for different servers can lose an entry (real but narrow). The tmp file is created 0600 by us, so "old perms" only applies to a stale tmp, and fsync is not needed for correctness here.
  - **Fix:** Add `static FILE: Mutex<()>` in oauth.rs and take `let _g = FILE.lock().unwrap();` at the top of `store` and `sign_out`. Skip the perms and fsync parts.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+3
  - **Risk:** low — std Mutex held only across sync load+save (no .await inside store/sign_out; refresh's async ONE mutex is separate, no lock-order issue; save does not take FILE, no re-entrancy). Use `.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner())` to avoid poison panics. Only protects within one process (two bone processes still race), acceptable.
  - **Benefit:** low — closes a narrow lost-update window between refresh and exchange.
  - **Call:** do
- [ ] 2.60 [checked] `crates/bone-server/src/unix.rs:29-48` — stale-socket check, remove and bind not atomic; two launches can orphan a core. `unix.rs:65,98` unlink removes whatever is at the path. Contradicts architecture.md:37.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — stale check (connect) then `remove_file` then bind is not atomic: two launches both see a stale socket, A binds, B removes A's fresh socket and binds, orphaning A. `unlink` removes whatever is at the path (a successor's socket too).
  - **Fix:** replace the connect-probe with an exclusive `flock` on `<sock>.lock` held in the Listener: `let lock = OpenOptions::new().create(true).write(true).open(lock_path)?; lock.try_lock().map_err(|_| AddrInUse "a server is already listening")?;` Whoever holds the lock owns the path, so any existing socket is stale: `remove_file` (ignore NotFound) then bind; keep the "exists and is not a socket" guard. Store `Mutex<Option<File>>` in place of the `removed` AtomicBool: `unlink` takes it, removes the socket only while holding it, then drops it (releases the lock so a successor can start during the idle grace; the architecture.md:37 loser-leaves story holds). `File::try_lock` is std 1.89: bump `rust-version` 1.88 -> 1.89. Never delete the lock file.
  - **Lines:** about -10/+16
  - **Risk:** medium — (a) after taking the flock, an OLD bone binary (no lock) still serving the same path would have its live socket removed; the current connect-probe is what protects the upgrade case, so keep the probe as a second check (AddrInUse if it connects) after the lock is held. (b) `File::try_lock` needs Rust 1.89: the proposal bumps workspace rust-version 1.88 -> 1.89 (bone-gui is already 1.95; nothing else pins 1.88; local toolchain is 1.98). (c) lock file `<sock>.lock` is never deleted (intended). (d) flock on exotic filesystems for a custom --listen path (NFS) may not exclude. Tests to re-run: crates/bone/tests/headless.rs (second listener fails, stale socket replaced), bone-gui multi_client.rs:337/460, wait_idle straggler path in main.rs:364.
  - **Benefit:** low — closes a rare two-simultaneous-launch race that today costs one orphaned core.
  - **Call:** do with care — add the connect-probe after the lock; verify `unlink` after wait_idle then successor bind works, and that Drop after unlink never removes a successor's socket.
- [ ] 2.70 [unverified] `crates/bone/src/main.rs:399` — each spawn truncates core.log (loser truncates winner's log). `main.rs:438` killed child not reaped. `main.rs:531` Windows wording on Unix EACCES.
  - **Verdict:** partly — `File::create` truncates, so a loser's launch wipes the winner's log (confirmed). The unreaped `child.kill()` at the deadline is moot because the launcher returns the error and exits. The wording is confirmed: Unix EACCES on connect prints the "older bone run as administrator" text.
  - **Fix:** open the log with `OpenOptions::new().create(true).append(true)`, record `let start = log_file.metadata().map_or(0, |m| m.len())`, and on early-exit read only `log` bytes after `start` for `why`. Reword the PermissionDenied message (~line 531) to drop the Windows-only clause: "(another user's, or run with elevated rights)". Skip the reap.
  - **Lines:** about -2/+6
  - **Risk:** low-medium — switching core.log to append makes it grow forever (each launch appends; with 2.66 retry spam much faster), so add a size cap (truncate at start when > ~1 MB, or rotate). The current non-append File::create also makes the surviving winner write into a sparse file after a loser truncates, so append is a real improvement. The `why` read must slice from the recorded start offset. Message text (main.rs:535) is not pinned by any test (grep'd); keep the Windows wording where cfg(windows). This core.log is the socket-dir one, distinct from the ~/.bone/core.log of docs/lua.md.
  - **Benefit:** low — loser no longer wipes the winner's log; accurate Unix error wording.
  - **Call:** do with care — O_APPEND + start-offset read + size cap; cfg-split the PermissionDenied wording instead of one merged string.
- [ ] 2.67 [unverified] `crates/bone-proto/src/transport.rs:175` — fallback `/tmp/bone3-$USER` accepted regardless of owner/mode.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed (low severity) — `DirBuilder::recursive.mode(0o700)` accepts a pre-existing `/tmp/bone3-$USER` made by another user with any mode, and the client connects to whatever socket is there.
  - **Fix:** do it once on the client side, which covers any directory: in `bone_proto::client::connect` after `UnixStream::connect`, reject a peer whose uid differs from ours: `if stream.peer_cred()?.uid() != unsafe { libc::geteuid() } { return Err(PermissionDenied.into()) }` (add `libc` as a unix-only dep of bone-proto; bone-lua already uses it). Server side needs nothing: its socket is 0600.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+8
  - **Risk:** medium — enforcing peer uid inside bone_proto::client::connect applies to every `--connect PATH`, reconnect() and spawn_core: legitimate sockets served via a root-owned proxy/bind mount/container (peer uid != euid) would be refused with PermissionDenied. attach() already maps PermissionDenied to the "another bone ... may not join" message (main.rs:531), which fits the default path. Adds libc dep to bone-proto (bone-android also consumes it; unix-only cfg needed).
  - **Benefit:** low — only matters on the /tmp/bone3-$USER fallback (Linux without XDG_RUNTIME_DIR) with a hostile local user.
  - **Call:** revise — check only for the default-socket path (in attach/spawn_core: parent dir owned by euid and not group/other-writable, or peer uid check gated to default_socket_path()), leave explicit --connect unchanged; or skip.
- [ ] 2.63 [unverified] `crates/bone-proto/src/transport.rs:118` — a non-UTF-8 line closes the connection (protocol.md says parse error).
  - **Verdict:** partly — a non-UTF-8 line does hit `Err(_) => return` and drops the connection, but protocol.md only promises -32700 for "not valid JSON-RPC", so this is a small spec gap.
  - **Fix:** in transport.rs `read_loop` replace the `from_utf8` match with `let line = String::from_utf8_lossy(&buffer);` (bad bytes become U+FFFD; broken framing then fails JSON parse and gets the existing -32700 reply, and the connection stays up).
  - **Lines:** about -4/+1
  - **Risk:** low — read_loop is shared by server and client; lossy decode only turns invalid bytes into U+FFFD inside a frame that then goes through normal JSON parse (-32700 on failure). No test pins the drop-on-invalid-UTF-8 behaviour found in transport.rs tests.
  - **Benefit:** low — matches protocol.md's parse-error promise; garbage no longer kills the connection.
  - **Call:** do
- [ ] 2.82 [unverified] `crates/bone-tui/src/terminal.rs:124,128` — panic hook pops keyboard flags never pushed; `enter()` errors leave raw mode on.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — terminal.rs `enter()` installs the panic hook with `restore(true)` before it knows keyboard enhancement was pushed, and any `?` after `enable_raw_mode()` returns without restoring.
  - **Fix:** Move the `set_hook` block below the `enhanced` detection and capture it: `restore(enhanced)`. Rename the body `setup()` and make `enter()` = `setup().inspect_err(|_| restore(false))` (no hook-leak concern: the hook is only installed after the fallible steps).
  - **Lines:** about -0/+4
  - **Risk:** low — the hook move and the `enhanced` capture are fine. As written, `setup().inspect_err(|_| restore(false))` is a mild regression. If enable_raw_mode fails because stdin or stdout is not a tty, restore() would write the mouse-off, bracketed-paste-off and LeaveAlternateScreen escapes to a piped stdout. If setup fails after PushKeyboardEnhancementFlags, restore(false) never pops the flags.
  - **Benefit:** low — an error path (non-tty or terminal setup failure) and a hook popping flags that were never pushed, which terminals ignore.
  - **Call:** do with care — on error call only `disable_raw_mode()` if raw mode was enabled, plus the keyboard pop if `enhanced`, not the full restore. Install the panic hook after the push, capturing `enhanced`.
- [ ] 2.21 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/scripting.rs:1179` — wait spec conversion error becomes Null, then an unrelated error.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `from_lua(&spec).unwrap_or(Json::Null)` (scripting.rs ~1179) hides the conversion error and later waits fall through to a generic "unknown wait".
  - **Fix:** `let spec = match from_lua(&spec) { Ok(s) => s, Err(e) => return self.finish(done, Err(mlua::Error::runtime(e.to_string()))) };` (fits into the 2.1 loop rewrite).
  - **Lines:** about -1/+4
  - **Risk:** medium — as proposed, a non-JSON-convertible wait spec (function/userdata inside e.g. bone.http body/headers) turns into finish(done, Err), failing the whole tool/hook; today it resumes the Lua caller with `(nil, "unknown wait: null")` (see Job::Resume Err path), which plugins can handle with `local r, err = bone.http(...)`. The fix would convert a recoverable nil,err into a hard failure of the hook/tool (hook errors become deny/failed turn).
  - **Benefit:** low — only improves the error message.
  - **Call:** revise — on from_lua error resume the coroutine with `(nil, <conversion error message>)` (same MultiValue shape as the session_op Err arm) instead of finishing it; fits the 2.1 loop as `args = ...; continue`.
- [ ] 2.20 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/scripting.rs:642` — Lua tool image paths read fully before any size check.
  - **Verdict:** partly — `read_tool_images` does `std::fs::read` unbounded, but the paths come from the user's own trusted Lua tool and the attachment store enforces the cap afterwards.
  - **Fix:** Cap before reading, as `attachments.rs:117` does: `if std::fs::metadata(&path).map_err(..)?.len() > bone_media::MAX_IMAGE_BYTES as u64 { return Err(format!("{path} exceeds the 20 MiB limit")) }`.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+4
  - **Risk:** low — as proposed, a metadata().len() check does not bound reads of special files (FIFO, /dev/zero report len 0 and std::fs::read then never ends or exhausts memory) and is racy (TOCTOU). Error text changes slightly (bone_media::normalize already rejects >20 MiB later, so no behavior beyond earlier failure). Runs on the Lua thread, so an unbounded read also stalls all Lua.
  - **Benefit:** low — tool paths are trusted; guards accidental huge files.
  - **Call:** revise — copy the read_file.rs/attachments.rs pattern: `File::open(&path)?.take(MAX_IMAGE_BYTES as u64 + 1).read_to_end(&mut buf)` then error if `buf.len() > MAX_IMAGE_BYTES`.
- [ ] 2.28 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/settings.rs:255` — `set_secret` returns Ok without saving when `providers` isn't an object; `settings.rs:42,277` rename without fsync, and 0600 only on create.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `set_secret` skips the insert when `providers` is not an object but still writes and returns Ok; `save` and `set_secret` rename without `sync_all`; 0600 is set only when the tmp file is created, so a stale tmp with other perms is renamed over the secrets.
  - **Fix:** `let Value::Object(m) = map else { return Err(format!("{}: \"providers\" is not an object", ...)) };` (de-nests the match). For the rest, add one `pub(crate) fn write_atomic(path, bytes, secret: bool)` (create tmp with `remove_file` first, mode 0600 if secret, `write_all`, `sync_all`, rename) and call it from `settings::save`, `set_secret`, oauth `save`, attachments, session `save_queue`, edit_file/write_file (items 2.43/2.49), replacing five hand-rolled copies.
  - **Lines:** about -35/+20 across files (this item alone about -8/+3)
  - **Risk:** medium — the providers-not-an-object part (return Err instead of silent Ok) is safe; set_secret has a single RPC caller (lib.rs:816). The proposed shared write_atomic bundled across settings, oauth, attachments, save_queue and edit_file/write_file is much riskier: sync_all on every settings.save adds fsync latency to frequent UI setting writes; the 0600 flag must not apply to tool writes; atomic-rename for edit_file/write_file would break symlink targets, ownership and file mode of user files (belongs to items 2.43/2.49, not this range). The shared `.settings.json.tmp` name already races across processes (remove-then-create does not fix it).
  - **Benefit:** low — secrets-file hygiene on crash.
  - **Call:** revise — do only the set_secret not-an-object Err; for settings/secrets (not tool writes) add `remove_file(tmp)` before create and `sync_all` before rename in secrets only (settings.json fsync optional); do not introduce the cross-crate helper in this item.
- [ ] 2.74 [unverified] `crates/bone-ui/src/lua.rs:862` (opt_define), `1207` (keymap_set), `lua/jobs.rs:44` (start_op), `lua.rs:2100` (popup_open) — callbacks stored before validation; leak on error.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — opt_define stores on_change before the choices checks can fail; keymap_set stores the Lua rhs before parse_sequence/set can fail; popup_open leaves the callbacks apply_win_spec stored when it errors; start_op's `flag("lines")?`/`opt("name")?` run after callbacks are stored (its own cleanup covers only the callback closure).
  - **Fix:** Validate first, store last. opt_define: move the `callback = ...store_callback` statement down to just before `own(...)`/insert. keymap_set: move `let seq = keys::parse_sequence(&key).map_err(err)?;` above the `match rhs` (set cannot fail after that, once 2.81's keymap.rs reorder is done). start_op: read `name`, `lines`, `buffer` flags before the callbacks. popup_open: on `apply_win_spec` Err call a shared `release_popup(lua, &p)?` (drops lines, on_key, keys; the same 6 lines popup_close has inline, which then calls it too).
  - **Lines:** about -8/+10
  - **Risk:** low — none found. Moving store_callback after the validation in opt_define, parse_sequence above the rhs match in keymap_set, and name/lines/buffer flag reads before the callbacks in start_op only reorder error paths (the only visible change is which error wins when two things are wrong). popup_open: the GUI reaches it through bone-gui nodeview::window_spec -> bone_ui dispatch, so it gets the cleanup too; popup_update is unaffected (it re-inserts p on error).
  - **Benefit:** low — closes small registry leaks that happen only on a failing call.
  - **Call:** do — keep the 2.81 keymap.rs reorder (ensure_context after parse) with it. In start_op compute `buffer` after the callbacks, because its default depends on on_stdout/on_stderr.
- [ ] 2.75 [unverified] `crates/bone-ui/src/lua.rs:535` — `drop_callback` leaves `callback_owner`; replaced/deleted keymaps never drop their callback.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `drop_callback(lua, id)` only clears the registry slot, never `callback_owner`; keymap_set/keymap_del also never drop the Lua callback of the mapping they replace or remove (only plugin unload does).
  - **Fix:** Make `Keymaps::set` return the replaced `Option<Action>` (`self.maps.insert(k, action)`) and `del` the removed `Action`; in keymap_set/keymap_del `if let Some(Action::Lua(id)) = old { drop_callback(...)?; }`. For the owner map change `drop_callback` to `(h, lua, id)` and add `h.owners().callback_owner.remove(&id)`; the two sites inside `with_api` closures (plugins.rs release_callbacks, jobs.rs release) remove the ids from `callback_owner` after the closure instead. Same signature change fixes remove_context leaking its Lua actions (drop them in keymap_context_del).
  - **Lines:** about -0/+14
  - **Risk:** medium — the proposed `drop_callback(h, lua, id)` signature change touches about 40 call sites: bone-ui lua.rs, panels.rs, input.rs, jobs.rs, plugins.rs, and bone-gui nodeview/ops.rs, nodeview/popups.rs, system.rs. The `with_api` closures (plugins.rs release_callbacks, jobs.rs release) have no `h`, so those sites need a different path. Other hazards: `Keymaps::set`/`del` change return type (keymap.rs tests call them). keymap_set/del dropping the Lua callback can strand a cloned `Action::Lua(id)` in `key_state.pending_action` (a prefix waiting on its timeout), so the later dispatch hits a nil callback and reports a spurious Lua error. It also double-drops during plugin unload (keymap_del, then release_callbacks), which is harmless.
  - **Benefit:** low — the leaked callback_owner entry is a u64 -> String per replaced or closed callback, and a replaced keymap's Lua function stays in the registry until unload.
  - **Call:** revise — skip the drop_callback signature change. Do the narrow version: in keymap_set/keymap_del only, drop the replaced or removed `Action::Lua(id)` and `h.owners().callback_owner.remove(&id)`, and clear `pending_action` if it equals that id. Leave the other drop_callback sites alone.
- [ ] 2.79 [unverified] `crates/bone-ui/src/lua.rs:2142` (row/col cast unclamped), `460,1494` (`Instant + ms` overflow), `lua/ui.rs:384` (ui_broken grows), `lua/processes.rs:243` (sparse array).
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — all four: `n as i32` on an i64 for row/col wraps (popup.rs `-n - 1` can also overflow on i32::MIN); `Instant::now() + Duration::from_millis(ms.max(0.0) as u64)` panics for huge or infinite ms (bone.ui.refresh_in(math.huge)); `ui_broken` gets "popup.{id}" inserted by `guarded` and only panels remove theirs; screen_table uses `l.set(j + 1, ..)` inside `if let Item::Text`, so a Fill item leaves a hole in the Lua array.
  - **Fix:** (1) apply_win_spec row/col: `n.clamp(i16::MIN as i64, i16::MAX as i64) as i32`. (2) One helper `fn deadline(ms: f64) -> Instant { Instant::now() + Duration::from_millis(ms.clamp(0.0, 86_400_000.0) as u64) }` replacing the two copies (lua.rs `ui_refresh_in`, `chat_refresh_in`). (3) popup_close (via release_popup from 2.74): `h.ui_broken().remove(&format!("popup.{}", p.id));`. (4) processes.rs screen_table: `l.push(...)` instead of `l.set(j + 1, ...)` and drop the `.enumerate()` index.
  - **Lines:** about -4/+8
  - **Risk:** low — none found. The i16 clamp also fixes place() in popup.rs, which does `(n as u16)` and `(-n - 1) as u16` and so wraps for |n| > 65535 (row=65536 lands on row 0). A 1-day cap on refresh_in is harmless, and NaN.clamp stays NaN and `as u64` gives 0. Removing "popup.{id}" from ui_broken in popup_close needs the release_popup helper from 2.74. screen_table push: terminal screens only produce Item::Text (term.rs), so the sparse-array fix is a no-op today, and mux.lua / terminal.lua never see a hole. mlua 0.11 has Table::push.
  - **Benefit:** low — hardening against math.huge, huge coordinates and a ui_broken set that grows.
  - **Call:** do
- [ ] 2.81 [unverified] `crates/bone-ui/src/popup.rs:135` (truncate before clamp), `jobs.rs:400` (fcntl unchecked), `keymap.rs:236` (context created before parse).
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — popup.rs: `lines.len() as u16` truncates before the `.min(area.height)`; jobs.rs: `libc::fcntl` result ignored (a failed O_NONBLOCK makes tokio's pipe read block a worker); keymap.rs `set` calls `ensure_context` before `parse_sequence`, so a bad key still creates the context.

  - **Lines:** about -0/+4
  - **Risk:** low — none found. The popup.rs min(u16::MAX) cut is trivial. keymap.rs: parse before ensure_context only changes whether a bad key leaves an empty context behind. For jobs.rs also check the F_GETFL result: a -1 there makes `-1 | O_NONBLOCK` pass all-ones flags to F_SETFL. attach_pty already returns io::Result and its caller handles Err.
  - **Benefit:** low — defensive, three tiny fixes.
  - **Call:** do
- [ ] 2.84 [unverified] `crates/bone-tui/src/lua.rs:124` — errors logged twice; `app.rs:391` log grows forever; chats never closed.
  - **Verdict:** partly — duplicate logging is real (lua_error pushes the full text to `log`, then `self.error()` pushes the short text again); `log` is an unbounded Vec; "chats never closed" is a missing feature (no close op exists), not a bug in this code.
  - **Fix:** In lua_error replace `self.error(...)` with setting `self.message = Some((short, Level::Error)); self.dirty = true;` so `log` gets only the full line (check tests that read `app.log.last()`). Cap the log with one helper `fn log_line(&mut self, s: String)` (push, and `if len > 2000 { drain(..1000) }`) used by info/error/lua_error. Drop the chats part.
  - **Lines:** about -1/+8
  - **Risk:** low — tests read app.log: reload_tests `last_log` expects messages from `error()`/`info()` ("Lua reload failed...", "core reload failed..."), not from lua_error, and tests.rs 4050/4327 use `.iter().any(contains)`. Setting message without pushing the short line leaves the full line in log, and `bone.api.log` (tests.rs:1047) still shows the traceback. Check any test asserting `app.log.last()` right after a lua_error. The cap in `log_line` makes `bone.api.log(n)` forget old entries, which is fine. The GUI keeps its own log and its lua_error never pushes the full text, so there is no parity issue.
  - **Benefit:** low — removes a duplicate log line and an unbounded Vec.
  - **Call:** do with care — run the bone-tui tests (tests.rs, reload_tests.rs). The cap is the part that matters; the dedupe is optional.

- [ ] 2.87 [unverified] `crates/bone-gui/src/flex.rs:208,644` — percentage max resolved against 0. `flex.rs:600` content_height ignores align_self/margin auto. `flex.rs:561` absolute children anchored to the border box.
  - **Verdict:** partly — percent max/min resolved against 0 is real (`measure` passes `base_w = avail.unwrap_or(0.0)` and `0.0` for heights, so `max_height = 50%` clamps to 0; same in content_height/content_width); content_height ignores `align_self` and `margin_auto` (uses `s.align`, no auto_w) which place honours; absolute children are anchored to the border box, but that is a documented-by-code choice, not a fault unless a bordered container shows it.
  - **Fix:** Use NaN as "no basis": `const NO_BASIS: f32 = f32::NAN;` pass it as clamp's base where 0.0 stands for unknown (measure's base_w when avail is None, the height clamp, content_height, content_width) since `f32::min/max` ignore NaN, a percentage bound then drops out instead of becoming 0. content_height: `let align = cs.align_self.unwrap_or(s.align); let auto_w = cs.margin_auto[1] || cs.margin_auto[3];` and `Align::Stretch if !auto_w`. Leave the absolute-anchoring alone.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+6
  - **Risk:** medium — NaN as "no basis" changes measurement wherever a percent bound meets a missing base. `measure` with avail=None today clamps `max_width: "78%"` and `"45%"` to 0 width. Users: views.lua bubbles (78%), tray.lua titles (45%), overlays.lua (90%/80%), elements/nodes.lua images (100%). After the fix those measure at natural width when measured unconstrained (scroll rows via content_width, shrink-to-fit parents, aspect ratio). That can shift chat and tray layout. NaN is safe in clamp (f32::min/max ignore NaN) but Dim::Mix loses its px part too. No Lua sets a percent max_height. The content_height align_self/margin_auto fix matches place() and is safe.
  - **Benefit:** low — mostly latent bugs: nothing in the repo uses a percent height bound, and the width cases only misbehave in unconstrained measurement.
  - **Call:** do with care — crates/bone-gui, so ask first. Land the content_height align_self/auto_w part on its own; do the NaN part only with before/after full-window screenshots of chat bubbles, the tray, the overlays and the settings view.

- [ ] 6.3 [checked] `crates/bone-proto/src/message.rs:145` — `match self.clone()` deep-copies every outgoing message. Also `client.rs:62` `Event::parse` clones params; `server/src/lib.rs:74` clones per connection.
  - **Verdict:** partly — the `Message::serialize` `self.clone()` (`message.rs:145`) is real and runs for every outgoing message, including big payloads such as transcript/session-load responses; deltas are tiny. `Event::parse` clones params on every received event, but deltas are small. `server/lib.rs:74` is tokio `broadcast` giving each receiver its own clone, so it is inherent.
  - **Fix:** Fix only the serialize clone. Make `Raw` Deserialize-only and add a small `#[derive(Serialize)] struct RawRef<'a>` holding `Option<&'a RequestId/&'a str/&'a Value/&'a RpcError>` with the same skip attrs. Then `match self { .. }` by reference. Optionally make `Event::parse` use `N::Params::deserialize(self.params.as_ref().unwrap_or(&NULL))` with a `static NULL: Value = Value::Null`, which drops the Value clone (-1/+2). Leave the server broadcast clone.
  - **Lines:** about -6/+16
  - **Risk:** low — only wire shape matters: RawRef must keep the same field order and skip_serializing_if on Option<&T>, "result": null must stay emitted (Some(&Value::Null) does), jsonrpc as &'static str; Raw keeps its present/present_id deserializers and only loses Serialize + skip attrs. codec.rs:8 is the sole serializer (to_string), so the existing message.rs round-trip tests cover it. Event::parse swap is safe: Notification::Params is DeserializeOwned (methods.rs:120) so &Value deserializer works and errors stay serde_json::Error; `static NULL: Value` is const and Sync.
  - **Benefit:** low — removes one deep clone per outgoing socket/stdio message; only large session-load/transcript responses matter, deltas are tiny (in-process connections never serialize).
  - **Call:** do — serialize fix only (cheap, mechanical); Event::parse tweak optional.
- [ ] 6.8 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/agent.rs:1041` — `messages_from_lua` O(new × saved) reasoning matching with allocations. `agent.rs:990` `list()` always clones. `agent.rs:587,819` hook events built without `has_hook`. `session.rs:350` context cloned 2–3× per call. `compact.rs:370` tool schemas serialized per call.
  - **Verdict:** partly — (a) `messages_from_lua` O(new x saved) matching plus `key()` cloning Values: hook-only, small N (reasoning items only); leave the algorithm, but `key` can return `Option<&Value>` (make it a `fn`) to drop the allocations (-1/+2). (b) `list()` clone: hook/Lua-only, dwarfed by Lua marshalling; leave. (c) Hook events built without `has_hook`: confirmed and hot. `agent.rs:587` (`message`, per model call), `~819` (`tool_call`, plus `mcp.describe`) and `~938` (`tool_result`) always build their events, so they copy the full tool output, the tool-call arguments and the base64 tool images even when nothing is registered. (d) `session.context()` clone: per model call, a memcpy of the transcript, dwarfed by the network and the provider JSON build; leave (the extra `context()` at `agent.rs:608` is only used for a token count and is not worth an API change). (e) `tool_chars` `to_string()` per model call: tens of microseconds; leave.
  - **Fix:** Gate the three events with the existing `self.has_hook(name)` using the same `.then(|| json!{..})` pattern as `request()`: `let ev = self.has_hook("tool_result").then(|| json!{..});` then `match ev { Some(ev) => self.hooks(..).await, None => Ok(None) }`. Do the `key()` borrow fix too. Leave the rest.

  - **Risk:** low — agent.hooks() already returns Ok(None) when !has_hook, so a skipped event build is unobservable; has_hook is the same set hooks() checks and request() already uses the same `.then()` pattern. Care points: (1) tool_call: build `mcp.describe` inside the closure too and keep `let mut ev`/`args = ev["arguments"].clone()` branch only for Some; (2) tool_result: `hook_args = args.clone()` (agent.rs ~902) is also unconditional and is needed only when the hook exists, gate it too or the saving is half; (3) message event: `content/reasoning/calls` are moved after, so build via closure borrowing them, then `match ev { Some(ev) => self.turn_hooks(..).await?, None => None }` must keep the `?` and the Some(ev) rewrite block. key() fn: returns Option<&Value>, comparison `key(o) == key(item)` compiles with item being &mut (reborrowed in find closure, `*item = clone` after find returns).
  - **Benefit:** low — copies a tool output (size-capped) / args / small message per call; microseconds against network + tool run; description's "hot" overstates it. key() change only matters with a request hook.
  - **Call:** do with care — gate the three events and hook_args as above (small, safe); key() fn change optional; verify with agent hook tests (bone-core scripting/agent tests with message/tool_call/tool_result hooks registered and not registered).
- [ ] 6.9 [unverified] `crates/bone-core/src/index.rs:267` — `retain` uses Vec::contains (O(n×m)); `370` FTS delete by UNINDEXED column (full scan); `286` new connection per query; `228` sync work with no new bytes. `session.rs:206` reindex under the session lock on every push.
  - **Verdict:** partly — all cold or startup-only. `catch_up` runs once per core start on a background thread (`lib.rs:480`); `push` reindex is once per transcript message, not per frame. Only two sub-points are worth a few lines.
  - **Fix:**
  - `retain` (index.rs:267): confirmed O(sessions x files), but startup-background only. Build `let keep: HashSet<&str> = ids.iter().map(String::as_str).collect();` and filter with `!keep.contains(id.as_str())`.
  - `sync` with no new bytes (index.rs:228): confirmed. Every `catch_up` session takes an IMMEDIATE write transaction and does an `INSERT OR REPLACE files` plus `UPDATE sessions` even when `offset == len`. After `len` is read, add `if known.is_some() && offset == len && renamed.is_none() { return Ok(()); }` (dropping the tx rolls back). That turns startup catch-up for N unchanged sessions into N cheap reads.
  - FTS delete by UNINDEXED `session_id` (clear(), index.rs:370): a full scan, but only on `remove`, a shrunk file, or `retain` of vanished sessions. Leave.
  - New read-only connection per `query` (index.rs:286): deliberate (separate reader, progress-handler deadline, doesn't contend with the writer lock); costs ~100us per `store/query`. Leave.
  - Reindex under the session lock on every push (session.rs:206): one small WAL transaction per message (synchronous=NORMAL, no fsync). Moving it off-lock adds ordering and complexity for sub-millisecond cost. Leave.
  - **Lines:** about -1/+7
  - **Risk:** low — retain HashSet is pure (index.rs:267, sole caller session.rs:716 catch_up). The `sync_once` early return (index.rs:185) skips only an INSERT OR REPLACE of identical values plus an UPDATE sessions that sets messages=seq/updated_at=max(...) to values already stored, so nothing observable changes; `renamed.is_none()` keeps the catch_up rename path (session.rs:711, `saved(id,"title")`) writing, so titled sessions get no savings but stay correct. Known==None (new file, even len 0) still falls through, so the existing tests (index.rs:502 writer-wait, :552 corrupt) are unaffected. Dropping the IMMEDIATE tx rolls back (rusqlite default). Edge: a sessions row deleted out-of-band while `files` row remains would no longer be touched, but the old UPDATE could not recreate it either.
  - **Benefit:** low — background startup only; fewer write txns and an O(n) retain, no UI effect.
  - **Call:** do with care — keep the early return placed after `len` is read and gated on `known.is_some() && offset == len && renamed.is_none()` (offset must be the possibly-unchanged value, i.e. before any clear); the HashSet retain is a safe independent win.

One miss: item (f) omits reason "clear" which compact.rs:735 also emits on session/updated; protocol.md:50/136 already document "clear". No golden test reads doc comments.

## P4 — no user-visible effect (maintainer cleanup)

Rule violations, dead code, duplication. Worth doing for the codebase; users won't notice.

- [x] 3.12 [checked] `crates/bone-core/src/agent.rs:39` — default system prompt in Rust; architecture says defaults are Lua.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — agent.rs:39 `DEFAULT_SYSTEM_PROMPT` duplicates runtime/core/defaults.lua:4 and is dead whenever defaults.lua runs. The two texts have already drifted.
  - **Fix:** Delete the const (agent.rs:39-46). In `system_prompt()` (agent.rs:~1000) use `.unwrap_or("")` (or `unwrap_or_default()`). Nothing else references the const. The only test on this (`default_system_prompt_points_at_the_docs`) loads defaults.lua through `Harness::with_lua_data`, so it is unaffected.
  - **Lines:** about -9/+0
  - **Risk:** low — const used only at agent.rs:1000. core/defaults.lua always runs for a real core (scripting.rs:1380) and sets system_prompt as a string, so the const is dead in production. Only behaviour change: a user who does `bone.config.system_prompt = nil` in core.lua (or deletes the defaults override) now gets an empty base, i.e. system = "\n\nWorking directory: ..." (leading blank lines; consider trimming) instead of the older Rust text. Tests with `system_prompt: None` (agent_tests.rs:128, bone-server/tests/echo.rs:27) assert only suffixes / contains, not the default text; scripting/tests.rs:51 checks the Lua default; the docs test (agent_tests.rs:1601) goes through defaults.lua. No model-behaviour change by default (the Lua prompt is what models already receive).
  - **Benefit:** medium — removes a drifted second copy of the prompt and the rule violation.
  - **Call:** do — use unwrap_or_default / "" and format so an empty base doesn't produce leading newlines; run bone-core + bone-server tests.
- [ ] 3.1 [checked] `crates/bone-core/src/agent.rs:650` — core hard-codes the `"subagent"` tool name for batching. Let a tool declare a parallel group.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — agent.rs:650-654 matches `"subagent"` by name to give it an uncapped, separate batch.
  - **Fix:** Replace the name test with a per-tool cap. Change `Tool::parallel(&self) -> bool` to `-> Option<usize>` (None = serial, Some(n) = batch limit, 0 = unlimited). LuaTool parses `parallel = true` as `Some(MAX_PARALLEL)` and `parallel = <number>` as `Some(n)` (scripting.rs:1574 region). The agent.rs loop becomes `let cap = self.parallel(&calls[i]); while end < len && self.parallel(&calls[end]) == cap && (cap == Some(0) || end - i < cap)`. The subagent plugin changes `parallel = true` to `parallel = 0`. Calls with different caps never share a batch, which is today's behaviour. The built-in read tools return `Some(MAX_PARALLEL)`.
  - **Lines:** about -6/+14 (Rust), plus 1 line in the subagent plugin (separate repo)
  - **Risk:** medium — version skew, not code. Callers of Tool::parallel: agent.rs:644/652/775-782, scripting.rs:613 (LuaTool), runtime.rs:217 (parallel_tools HashSet), mcp.rs:1198, read_file.rs:51, tools/mod.rs:142, agent_tests.rs:1327/2081 (pass bool `parallel=`). All mechanical. Real regression: new core + currently installed subagent plugin (~/.bone/plugins/subagent/core.lua:579 and bone-catalog `parallel = true`) maps to Some(8): subagent calls now batch together with read_file/web_search/mcp reads and are capped at 8 per batch, so `subagent.max_concurrent` > 8 or 0 (unlimited) silently stops working until the plugin is updated to `parallel = 0`. Reverse skew is safe (old core reads `parallel = 0` as truthy since get::<Option<bool>> only checks == Some(true) on mlua coercion; verify). Also scripting.rs:1520 must change from `== Some(true)` to accept bool|integer, and bad values (negative, float, string) need a defined error. docs/lua.md:295,308 name the subagent exception and must be reworded.
  - **Benefit:** medium — removes the last plugin name hard-coded in core and generalises "uncapped batch" to any tool.
  - **Call:** do with care — ship plugin bump first (or same release with min_bone_version in subagent manifest); keep `parallel=true` meaning Some(8); run bone-core agent_tests (parallel_* at ~2081-2150, child at 1327) and add no new tests unless asked; update lua.md.

  - **Verdict:** confirmed — elements/nodes.lua:171 (base) requires into the `desktop` library. markdown.lua's only dependency is `bone.nodes.style`, so it is already base-level code.
  - **Fix:** `git mv runtime/lua/bone/desktop/markdown.lua runtime/lua/bone/nodes/markdown.lua`. Update the path in bone-lua/src/lib.rs:64. Change `require("bone.desktop.markdown")` to `bone.nodes.markdown` in elements/nodes.lua:171, desktop/views.lua:11, pick.lua:8, help.lua:10, tools.lua:13, and in docs/customizing/customizing-gui.md:350. Pure rename with no new code. Third-party GUI libraries that copied the desktop path would break, so mention it in release notes.

  - **Risk:** medium — pure rename but old path has real users: (1) ~/.bone/runtime/lua/bone/desktop/tools.lua is a user override that does require("bone.desktop.markdown") (line 13); after the rename the embedded file is gone and the override fails at load, breaking the GUI tool views. (2) bone-catalog/plugins/subagent/gui.lua:6 hard-requires it (also installed at ~/.bone/plugins/subagent/gui.lua); (3) bone-catalog files look.lua:1123 pcalls it and degrades to plain text. (4) user/third-party GUI libraries made earlier copy desktop/markdown.lua themselves (create_tui_library copies RUNTIME wholesale) so full copies keep working, but partial libraries fall through to the embedded runtime and break. Also lib.rs:64 embed list must be updated (include_str! fails the build if missed), plus docs/customizing/customizing-gui.md:350 and the 5 in-repo require sites (elements/nodes.lua:171, desktop/views.lua:11, pick.lua:8, help.lua:10, tools.lua:13). Tests/Lua tests do not reference the path.

  - **Call:** do with care — git mv + update lib.rs, but leave runtime/lua/bone/desktop/markdown.lua as a 1-line shim `return require("bone.nodes.markdown")` (add to RUNTIME list) so ~/.bone/runtime overrides, subagent/files plugins and old libraries keep working; remove the shim only after catalog plugins migrate.

- [ ] 5.14 `bone-tui/src/app.rs:60,296` — `LuaSide` 22 fields swapped by hand; one `lua: LuaSide` field + one `mem::replace`.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — 22 fields declared in `LuaSide` (app.rs:60) and again in `App`, plus 21 hand-written swaps in `swap_lua_side`.
  - **Fix:** Do what the GUI already does (`self.lua: LuaState` in bone-gui/src/lua.rs). Make `App` hold `pub lua_side: LuaSide` (rename the inner `lua: Option<Lua>` to `state`, or call the field `side` to keep `self.lua` readable), delete the 22 duplicate field declarations and doc comments, and make `swap_lua_side` one `std::mem::replace(&mut self.side, side)` plus the three rev bumps. About 150 mechanical `self.keymaps` -> `self.side.keymaps` renames across bone-tui (theme 28, spinner 13, panels 13, lua_errors 12, rest under 8 each); `input` has 300+ textual hits that are mostly other types, so grep per-field with the type in mind.
  - **Lines:** about -55/+10 (plus ~150 rename-only lines).
  - **Risk:** medium — compiler-checked and equivalent to the GUI's `self.lua: LuaState`, but wide: ~35+ sites for theme/keymaps alone plus tests.rs, panel.rs, plugins.rs, ui.rs, lua.rs (`impl Host for App` accessors return `&mut self.keymaps`, etc.). Check: (1) `App::new` (app.rs:217-285) initialises these fields with explicit values that must equal `LuaSide::default()` (Theme::default(), Keymaps::default(), jobs/spinner Default) -- they do today, but a new field with a non-Default init would silently diverge; (2) the 21 swaps become one mem::replace: same semantics; keep `views_rev/opts_rev/dirty` bumps; (3) `pub` fields on App (keymaps, theme, popups, panels, spinner, input, dynamic_options, jobs, ui_broken, colors_name, panel_focus) are only used inside bone-tui (no `app.keymaps` etc. in other crates; bone tests use Headless) so no external break; (4) field-splitting borrows (`&mut self.side.theme` + `&mut self.side.popups`) still disjoint, but any method call on `self.side` while another field is borrowed would not be. Reload path `reload_user_config` uses `swap_lua_side(Default::default())`, unchanged. `pub lua: Option<Lua>` rename to `side.state`: `app.lua` hits in lua.rs need care (grep `\.lua\b` also matches other things).
  - **Benefit:** medium — removes the "add a field in 3 places or reload silently keeps old state" trap (22 fields x 3 sites).
  - **Call:** do with care — do it in its own commit, rename field-by-field with the compiler, run bone-tui tests (reload_tests.rs, tests.rs) and a manual /reload; land before 5.13j/5.15 which touch the same files.
- [ ] 5.20 Shell tool: "read" == "status"; background spelled three ways; stdin "pipe" sentinel; six copy-pasted id checks; `far()` 86400s sentinel; STDERR_LINES keeps 20, uses 1.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — all six sub-items hold (shell.rs:115-215, :972-1098; STDERR in mcp.rs:42,609; `read` and `status` share one arm; `mode=start`, `background=true`, `action=start` all mean start).
  - **Fix:** (1) Make `action` a serde enum (`rename_all = "snake_case"`, `#[serde(alias = "read")] Status`), so unknown actions fail in `typed_args` and `read` stays accepted but is no longer a second documented action; drop `read` from the schema enum. (2) Replace the six `args.id.as_deref().ok_or(...)` blocks with one `let id = || args.id.as_deref().ok_or(format!("id is required for {}", action))` (or resolve the id once for all id-taking actions). (3) Keep `action=start` and `mode=start` only; delete `background` (the schema already calls it "Compatibility alias") and compute `start` from the enum. (4) `stdin: "pipe"` sentinel: make it `stdin: true` (pipe) or a string (text) with a two-variant untagged enum, so the literal text "pipe" can be sent. (5) Delete `far()`: a helper `async fn at(t: Option<Instant>) { match t { Some(t) => sleep_until(t).await, None => std::future::pending().await } }` lets the three timed select arms lose their `, if x.is_some()` guards and the 86400 s fake deadline. (6) mcp.rs: the stderr tail is `Arc<Mutex<VecDeque>>` capped at 20 and only `.back()` is read; keep `Arc<Mutex<Option<String>>>` of the last line and delete `STDERR_LINES`.
  - **Lines:** about -45/+15.
  - **Risk:** high (items 3 and 4) / low (2, 5, 6) — (3) Deleting `background` from `Args`: serde ignores unknown fields, so a model, saved prompt or Lua wrapper still sending `background:true` would silently run the command FOREGROUND with the 120 s default timeout, killing long-lived servers; keep it as an accepted undocumented alias (drop from schema only). Same for `mode`/`action=start` -- both are documented to the model (schema + description say `mode="start"`; tools/tests.rs:406,547 use mode start). (4) `stdin: "pipe"` is documented in the schema/description (`stdin="pipe"` and `data`); changing to bool/untagged enum breaks the contract with existing models, changes the JSON schema type (string -> boolean|string, which strict-schema providers may reject), and a literal text "pipe" is an edge nobody hits. (1) action enum: unknown actions would fail in `typed_args` with a serde message instead of `unknown shell action "x"` (check tests/other callers for that text; none found in crates), `read` must keep working via alias (models use it). (2) id checks: today's messages differ ("id is required for this action" for status/read, "for wait/kill/write/close_stdin"); one closure keeps per-action text via `action` name; fine. (5) far(): select! arms with `at(None)` = pending() are equivalent to the 86400 s sentinel with guard removed, but the kill_at arm has a compound guard (state/pipe_open/status/group_alive) that must stay; `deadline` arm must still be guarded by `kill_at.is_none()`. (6) stderr last-line: safe (only `.back()` is read, mcp.rs:630); note an empty trailing line stays last as today. No catalog plugin builds shell tool args (plugins/shell is the `!cmd` UI, not the tool).
  - **Benefit:** medium — (1),(2),(5),(6) remove real clutter; (3),(4) are API cleanup with user-visible risk.
  - **Call:** do with care — do (2), (5), (6) and (1) with `read` alias; skip (3) deletion of `background` (only de-document it) and skip (4); coordinate with 5.13g (same function).

(g) api_info().os == "windows" is safe (lib.rs:531 std::env::consts::OS); dropping the USERPROFILE branch is safe because crates/bone/src/main.rs:156 sets HOME and the GUI is linked into the same binary. No other plugin uses util.trim/pad/plain_error/home.

The GUI copy survives (it is the better diff). `desktop/tools.lua` appends ", N failed" itself; `ui/views.lua` deletes its prefix/suffix `diff_lines` and the batch loop at ~618 and calls `diff_rows(args, { context = 0 })` so the TUI look stays; `diff_view` already renders sign " " rows. - [ ] 5.9 Three UTF-8 truncators: `compact.rs:425`, `tools/mod.rs:218`, `bone-ui/src/text.rs:248`.
  - **Verdict:** partly — `compact::clip`, `clip_tail` and `tools::truncate_middle` are one byte-middle family; `bone-ui/src/text.rs:248` truncates by display columns and is a different function (reject).
  - **Fix:** One `clip_middle(s, head, tail, marker: impl FnOnce(omitted: usize) -> String)` in a new `bone-core/src/text.rs` that owns the char-boundary walk. `clip(text,max)` = `clip_middle(text, max*3/4, max/4, " […] ")`; `clip_tail` = head `max/4`, tail `max*3/4` (its `head >= tail` fallback is unreachable when len > max); `truncate_middle` passes the "[... N bytes omitted ...]" marker. Put the boundary helpers there too and use them for the other hand-rolled `is_char_boundary` loops in `shell.rs:376,741,756,917`. If the MSRV (`rust-version = "1.88"`) is bumped to 1.91, `str::floor_char_boundary/ceil_char_boundary` replace every loop and the helper file shrinks further.
  - **Lines:** about -40/+20
  - **Risk:** low — copies differ subtly: `clip` returns the text when `len <= max` but head+tail = max*3/4 + max/4 can be < max (e.g. max=7: 5+1=6), whereas `truncate_middle` tests `len <= head+tail`; a helper keyed on head+tail would clip strings of length max that `clip` leaves alone. `clip_tail` has the `head >= tail` -> clip fallback (unreachable for len>max) and `max==0` -> "". truncate_middle's marker reports `t - h` bytes and uses "\n\n[... N bytes omitted ...]\n\n" (tools/tests.rs:172 pins boundary behavior; compact.rs:779 pins clip). shell.rs boundary loops (376,741,756,917) have different directions (backward vs forward) so only fold the ones that match. Compact summaries feed the model, so a one-byte change alters prompts but no test pins exact output beyond the two above.
  - **Benefit:** low — ~-20 lines net, new module in bone-core.
  - **Call:** do with care — helper takes an explicit `max` pre-check (callers keep their `len <= limit` early return) and keep private wrapper names `clip`/`clip_tail`/`truncate_middle` so tests compile; leave shell.rs loops unless identical.

- [ ] 5.21 Lua modules register `bone.on` at require time without releasing ids on reload (`agents.lua`, `timing.lua`, `menu.lua`, `background.lua`, `sidebar.lua`, `tools.lua`, `tray.lua`); side effects in render (`sidebar.lua:405`, `tray.lua:68`, `composer.lua:40`).
  - **Verdict:** partly — the headline premise is rejected for the runtime modules; the render side effects are confirmed.
  - **Fix:** Reload builds a fresh Lua state and swaps the whole `LuaSide` (autocmds included, 5.14 shows that), so runtime modules calling `bone.on` at require time start with no old handlers; plugins are covered by `owned` (released on unload). The `bone.off` guards in usage.lua:57-61 (state kept on `bone._standard_status`, a field of the fresh `bone` table) and terminal.lua:706 are therefore dead or defensive; delete usage.lua's `if state.events then ... end` and the `state.events` list. Side effects in render do exist: tray.lua:285 (`ui.page = ...; focus()` inside the draw), sidebar.lua:~405 (rename ends and sends a request while building nodes), composer.lua:~40 (`menu.last_text/offset/selected` reset in `suggestions()`). The tray one is the real hazard; move it to the existing `prompt/changed`/process handler. Others: leave (they read-then-reset view state; moving them to events adds code). Line numbers in the review have drifted.
  - **Lines:** about -8/+6.
  - **Risk:** low (usage.lua deletion) / medium (tray move) — usage.lua `state.events`/`bone.off` block: reload swaps the whole Lua state (TUI reload_user_config, GUI likewise) so `bone._standard_status` is fresh and the block is dead; deleting is safe (no package.loaded resets in runtime besides terminal.lua:706 and window.lua:614). Tray: `ui.page = filled()[1]; focus()` inside the render derives state every frame and self-heals when the last row of a page disappears (jobs end from many sources: processes, queue, agents); moving it to `prompt/changed` or a process handler would leave an empty page / stale keymap context until some unrelated event, and keymap.focus ordering during draw is relied on. No repro of an actual bug is given. sidebar/composer side effects: review itself says leave.
  - **Benefit:** low — dead-code removal; tray hazard unproven.
  - **Call:** do with care — delete the usage.lua events bookkeeping only; skip the tray move unless a concrete bug (flicker/loop) is reproduced; if done, verify page falls back when a job ends with the tray unfocused, in both TUI and GUI.

Leave the rest (providers could move to their own module but that only moves lines). (b) setup.lua: four copies of `st.busy, st.note = false, {msg,true}; return redraw()` in `subscribe` -> `local function fail(msg)`; lift the model-picker block in `on_key` (l.405-425) into `pick_model(k, row)`. Leave the rest. (c) catalog.lua M.page: leave (flat, cohesive; only on_key is long). (d) paint.rs `paint()`: it already has siblings `input_ui`/`image_ui`; extract `text_ui` (the Kind::Text arm, ~55 lines) and `sense_node` (pointer/focus/hover block, ~110 lines, returns hovered/pressed/sensed), and bundle `fonts`+`lists` into one `PaintCtx` to drop the 9-parameter signature and its `allow(too_many_arguments)`. (e) mod.rs `view_ui`: extract `schedule(fx)` (size/rev/wake/expiry/dirty, l.631-668) and `tree_or_fallback(...)` (l.695-727); drop the manual `drop(_span)` pairs by scoping. (f) list.rs `realize_list`: extract a pure `first_visible(heights, gap, offset) -> (first, top, seen)` + `pin_index(...)` and `ListState::sync(l, est, width)` (top 25 lines); share a `Realize{fonts,base,sink,rebuilt}` struct with `realize` (also kills its allow). (g) shell.rs `run_job`: extract `open_reader(master, process, pid)` (the cfg'd Master match, 30 lines, 3 repeated kill+fail exits) and `spawn_stdin_writer(child)`; the three identical cancel arms (`state=..; terminate(pid); kill_at=..`) -> one `stop(state, grace)` closure; see 5.20 for `far()`. (h) render.rs `draw_prompt`: extract `row_spans(row, marks, selection, theme)` (the per-char style loop, ~50 lines) and `draw_side_borders`; both are pure. (i) bone-lua `new_state`: extract `install_fs`, `install_docs`, `install_json` next to the existing `install_state`; pure moves, makes the function ~60 lines. (j) app.rs: see 5.14; also move `tui_health` (145 lines) and the mouse/chat-scroll group (l.1316-1475) into their own `impl App` files like panel.rs/plugins.rs do; no code change. (k) plugins/files/gui.lua: the hand-written line editor (l.454-793, with undo/redo) is a separate concern: move to `plugins/files/lua/files/editor.lua`; or, if losing undo and line numbers is acceptable, replace it with the native multiline `text_input` node (sidebar.lua already uses one) and delete ~250 lines (user decision). (l) desktop/tools.lua `card()`: make the per-tool branches a table `BODY[name] = function(item, a, out, ok, expanded, extra) -> body, paras`, with subagent returning an already padded column; this deletes the `report`/`under` juggling at the end (l.527-535).
  - **Lines:** about -90/+60 for the real dedups (a,b,d-h,l); the pure moves (i,j,k-move) are ~0 net.
  - **Risk:** medium — mostly pure moves, but sub-items hide differences. (a) settings_page.lua: the two delete asks differ: `paction delete` (l.590) also does `st.prov, st.sel = nil, 1` before reload, the `d` key (l.742) does not (a sel reset there would jump the list cursor); so `delete_provider(name, then)` needs the post-step passed in. A shared PFIELDS table would erase per-context differences: new-draft vs existing rows have different field ORDER (name,base_url,model,type,key,env vs model,base_url,type,...), different `desc` for URL/Key/Key variable, and existing rows carry dynamic `changed`/`value`/`choices` (api, reasoning_effort, replay_reasoning tri-state). (b) setup.lua `fail(msg)`: only 3 real copies (l.367,370,377, all `false`); l.357 is `true`, different. (d,e,f) touch crates/bone-gui nodeview Rust, which memory "gui-changes-lua-only" says to ask before editing; also working tree has uncommitted crates/bone-gui/src/native.rs and untracked vendor/gpui. (g) shell.rs `stop(state, grace)` closure that mutates state/kill_at will conflict with the select! `if kill_at.is_none()` guards (borrowck), and `far()` is also removed by 5.20, so the two overlap on the same lines; Windows cfg branches (Master match, `cfg!(unix)` at the child.wait arm: EXIT_GRACE vs KILL_GRACE differ) cannot be compile-checked here. (h) draw_prompt: `spec` borrows app, `win` borrows app.windows mutably, `theme` re-borrowed twice; extraction of row_spans needs care. (j) overlaps 5.14/5.15 on app.rs/lua.rs. (k) plugins/files in bone3 is an old divergent copy (869-line gui.lua; the live catalog plugin is ~/projects/bone-catalog/plugins/files with ui.lua 5264 lines and its own editor), so editing it affects nothing shipped. No catalog plugin uses settings_page/setup internals except `require("bone.setup").open` (tuis/*/defaults.lua) and `bone.desktop.tools` `target`/`labels` (toolstrip strip.lua); (l) card() refactor must keep those exports and public fields.
  - **Benefit:** low — mostly size cosmetics; real dedups are ~10 lines (delete ask, fail helper, schedule/pin helpers).
  - **Call:** do with care — only (a) delete_provider with `then` param, (b) fail(), (h)/(i) pure extractions, (f)/(e) only if user OKs touching bone-gui Rust; skip PFIELDS table, skip (k) (stale copy), (l) only with desktop screenshot check, do (g) after/with 5.20 in one change and check `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` if available; sequence 5.13j after 5.14.
- [ ] 5.5 Provider skeleton: three `stream()` functions with different end-of-stream checks; `assistant_blocks` (anthropic.rs:179) ≈ `wire_assistant` (openai_responses.rs:122); delta accumulation, `wire_user`, data-URL and deferred tool images duplicated; generic HTTP helpers live in `openai.rs`. → `provider/http.rs` + one driver.
  - **Verdict:** partly — the HTTP/SSE skeleton and the user-message/deferred-image builders are duplicated; `assistant_blocks` vs `wire_assistant` only look alike.
  - **Fix:** (1) Move the generic part of `provider/openai.rs` (`post_events`, `send`, `Capture`, `encode`, `bearer`, `configured`, `merge`, `http_client`, `check_images`, MAX_ATTEMPTS/CONNECT_TIMEOUT, ~140 lines) into `provider/http.rs`; pure move, fixes the openai.rs-owns-everything dependency (`mod.rs` re-export `http_client` stays). (2) Add a tiny trait there: `trait Events { fn apply(&mut self, data:&str, on:DeltaSink)->Result<bool,ProviderError>; fn finish(self, done:bool, model:&str)->Result<Completion,ProviderError>; }` plus `async fn run(http,url,headers,body,acc,on_delta,model)` doing post_events then finish; each `stream()` shrinks to url + body + headers + `run(...)`. The end-of-stream checks stay per provider inside `finish(done)` (openai: finish_reason via check_end; responses: `completed` flag; anthropic: `!done` error), so behaviour is unchanged. (3) Share `wire_user` between openai.rs:356 and openai_responses.rs:182 as `user_message(content, images, text_type, |img| part)` and the "Images from tool call {id}" deferred-image loop in `wire_messages` (openai.rs:280) and `wire_input` (responses:73) as one `DeferredImages {push, flush}` helper. (4) Reject merging `assistant_blocks` and `wire_assistant`: same walk shape, but item types, ids, trailing-reasoning rules and insertion point all differ; a generic would need a trait with 6 hooks. Also skip a single mega-driver; (2) is enough.
  - **Lines:** about -70/+40
  - **Risk:** low for (1), medium for (2)/(3) — (1) pure move of post_events/send/Capture/bearer/configured/merge/http_client/check_images is safe (only callers: provider/mod.rs:8,153, openai_responses.rs:9, anthropic.rs:16; `pub(crate) use ...http_client` path must be updated). (2) the trait is a poor fit: anthropic `finish(model, prefix)` takes the history fingerprint, openai needs `check_end(done)` before `finish`, responses ignores `done` and relies on `completed`; the per-stream savings are ~3 lines each. (3) the deferred-image loops are identical in shape but openai.rs:280 skips empty assistant messages with `continue` before the flush check, and wire_user part shapes differ (`type:"text"` + `image_url:{url}` object vs `input_text` + `image_url` string); a shared helper must preserve both or request bodies change and break provider-side validation (no byte-level tests found; fake-provider matrix would catch some).
  - **Benefit:** low — (1) fixes a dependency smell, (2)/(3) save ~30 lines net.
  - **Call:** revise — do (1) only (pure move to provider/http.rs); skip (2) and (3).
- [ ] 5.6 Fake provider: handle/fault/Cut logic ×3, content-part walking ×2, sha256 hex ×2, hand-written SSE.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — Empty and Cut handling are identical in all three front ends; ErrorBody json is identical in two.
  - **Fix:** In `bone-fake-provider/src/lib.rs` `handle`: (a) add `State::reply(&self, conv, fault)` doing `backend.reply(conv).await.map_err(|e| ApiError(502,e))` and returning `Reply::default()` for `Fault::Empty`; the three `handle()`s call it and the `if fault == Some(Fault::Empty) { reply = Reply::default() }` blocks (completions:240, responses:339, messages:486) go away along with the `mut reply` params. (b) Apply Cut once after the front end returns, in lib.rs: `if fault == Some(Fault::Cut) { streamed.events.truncate(len.div_ceil(2).max(1)) }`, deleting completions:283, responses:478, messages:562 (Cut is exclusive with the early-return faults so semantics are the same). (c) One `sse_error(message)` helper for the ErrorBody payload shared by completions/responses; messages keeps its own event shape. (d) One `hex_sha256(impl AsRef<[u8]>)` in lib.rs replacing `State::mac`'s hex and `messages::fingerprint`. (e) The two `user_content` walkers differ only in part type names and error text; extract just `check_image_url(url, label)` (the data:image/http check). Leave the SSE format strings (`event:` vs `data:`) alone, only add `const DONE`.
  - **Lines:** about -45/+20
  - **Risk:** medium — (b) is wrong for completions: completions.rs:283 truncates BEFORE pushing the finish chunk, usage chunk and `data: [DONE]`, so it cuts half of the pre-finish events; applying Cut once on the final event list (as responses/messages do) keeps about half of a longer list, which for a short reply keeps the finish_reason chunk and breaks the "cut -> turn fails with 'ended'" assertion in crates/bone-fake-provider/tests/matrix.rs:351 for COMPLETIONS (and changes which fault-cut tests exercise a mid-tool-call cut). (a) Empty is identical in all three; (c)-(e) are safe (mac() hashes `secret:body`, fingerprint hashes v.to_string(); helper must only share the hex step; the two image checks differ in label/error text, keep the labels).
  - **Benefit:** low — test-support crate, ~-45 lines.
  - **Call:** revise — do (a),(c),(d),(e) and a shared `cut(&mut Vec<String>)` helper but keep calling it at each front end's current position (completions before the finish chunk).

Behaviour to decide: min width 10 (dock_resize) vs 20 (sessions); set `M.min` per panel or keep 20 for "sessions". The old "sessions-width" saved value is dropped (one-time reset). For the GUI, `nodes/window.lua:101-130` and `dock_resize.lua:10-30` share only the key/load/cache/save-on-release code (~12 lines); a shared `bone.dock_size(prefix)` is not worth a new module, leave.

(b) min width: dock_resize.min=10 vs sessions 20; the sessions page hit-testing assumes ~19+ columns ("Chats 2-6, Processes 10-18"), so keep 20 for "sessions". (c) saved value: sessions saves ctx.width (the width actually drawn after Rust's 20-column chat clamp, comment at sessions.lua:162), dock_resize saves the requested n so a drag past the clamp persists an oversized width. (d) previously saved `sessions-width` is dropped (one-time reset); docs/usage.md:60 and docs/lua.md:851 describe the setting and need a line. `bone.ui.sessions_width` default still works (user_size nil until dragged). Catalog tuis with their own ui/sessions.lua (notebook, zen, prism, codex-app) have no resize code, so they would gain drag from dock_resize only if it is loaded.
  - **Benefit:** low — ~-30 lines.
