- [ ] 5.15 "Is the library bare" recomputed in 4 places; "unknown option" detected by matching error text (`bone-tui/src/lua.rs:218`, `bone-gui/src/lua.rs:215`). Typed error.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — "bare" is derived in bone-tui/lua.rs:60, :179, :203 and bone-gui/lua.rs:182; the two `apply_*_settings` are copies, and they disagree (TUI skips "unknown option" only when bare, GUI skips it always).
  - **Fix:** Compute `bare` once when the Lua side loads and keep it as a field of the Lua side (it already travels into `load_user_files(dir, bare)`). Add `Host::is_bare()` and one `bone_ui::lua::apply_saved_options(host, "tui"|"gui", previous)` that replaces both loops. Replace the text match with a lookup: `options.rs` already knows the names (`Options::get` + dynamic map), so skip a saved key the frontend does not define when bare, else report it; no error-string matching and no `short_error` dependence.
  - **Lines:** about -40/+20.
  - **Risk:** medium — the two loops are NOT equivalent, and the review's unification silently changes GUI: GUI skips "unknown option" errors ALWAYS (gui/lua.rs:215), TUI only when bare (tui/lua.rs:218). Unifying to "skip only when bare" makes the GUI newly report `settings.json gui.<name>` errors (and push to load_errors/lua_errors, which the reload logic counts as "loaded with a Lua error") for any saved gui option whose plugin is disabled/uninstalled or removed from a library: a likely real regression for users with stale `gui.*` keys. Other differences: bare is derived differently (TUI `bone_lua::tui_library`; GUI `bone_lua::library(dir, Side::Gui)`, which honours gui-library and per-library gui dirs), so Host::is_bare must stay per-frontend; TUI re-reads the `library` file at apply time (update_settings, any time) while a cached field would reflect the library at Lua load (arguably more correct, but behavior change after `/tui use` before reload); apply timing differs (TUI before tui.lua, GUI after user files) so only the loop can be shared; error-context strings "settings.json tui.{name}" / "gui.{name}"; TUI skips null/unchanged identically. Replacing the text match with a name lookup must also cover dynamic options (`dynamic_options` map, which differs per frontend) and must still report wrong-type/invalid values; `Options::get` only knows 7 TUI-side built-ins (GUI options live elsewhere). Test bone-tui/tests.rs:1084 pins the "unknown option" text for `bone.o.nope`, so the error string itself must stay.
  - **Benefit:** low — ~20 lines saved, removes string matching.
  - **Call:** revise — share only the loop in bone_ui::lua with a `skip_unknown: bool` param (TUI passes bare, GUI passes true) to preserve current behavior; cache `bare` per Lua load; keep the "unknown option" string match or add a typed error only if every `bone.o` set path produces it.
- [ ] 5.17 `bone-ui/src/options.rs` — each option listed in 5 parallel places. `settings.rs:79` validate is a 90-line if-chain.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — each of the 7 built-in options appears in the struct, `Default`, `describe`, `NAMES`, `get` and `set` (options.rs:6-250). `settings.rs:79` (bone-core) `validate` is one 90-line if-chain, and its `["providers", _]` object arm re-checks, by hand, what the `["providers", _, field]` arm already checks per field.
  - **Fix:** Options: a small `macro_rules! options { name: ty = default, "desc"; ... }` that emits the struct, `Default`, `NAMES`, `describe`, `get` and `set` (with `prompt_max_height`'s `.max(1)` as an optional clamp); fields stay typed so no caller changes. Validate: pull the per-field match into `fn provider_field(path, field, value) -> Result`, and have the object arm loop `for (k, v) in p { provider_field(..)? }` then keep only the cross-field checks (model required, base_url/type required, api_key rejected). That deletes the 12-branch if-chain. Note: field arm accepts null, the object arm currently rejects a null `base_url`; the shared path makes them consistent.
  - **Lines:** about -70/+35.
  - **Risk:** high (validate part) / low (options macro) — Options macro: `Value` has only Bool/Number and fields mix bool/usize/u64 with `n as usize` casts, `.max(1)` clamp on prompt_max_height, and doc comments on fields; a macro needs per-type conversion hooks and attribute passthrough, and the error arms (`wrong type for`, `unknown option`) must keep text; `apply()` and the one test depend on them. Adding an option is rare, so macro obfuscation outweighs 5 touch points. validate(): the object arm (providers.<name> object) and field arm are NOT equivalent. (1) The object arm allows UNKNOWN keys (e.g. a Lua provider type's own options, `ProviderConfig.options` is "the whole table handed to a Lua provider"), the field arm rejects unknown fields (`_ => Err("a provider's settings are ...")`); looping `provider_field` over all keys would start rejecting `settings/set providers.x {type=..., custom=...}`. (2) The object arm does not check reasoning_effort/stream_usage at all; the field arm does, so previously accepted objects would be rejected. (3) null handling differs per key (object rejects null base_url/model/type/api_key_env, replay_reasoning, cache, headers/body; field accepts null = remove), and `model: null` passes `p.get("model").is_none()`. (4) Error text and ORDER differ (`{path}.{e}` vs `{path}: {e}`); agent_tests.rs:2816-2822 pin "needs a model", "needs a base_url", "secrets.json" for objects, and settings.rs tests pin supports_images=1/replay_reasoning="true" objects as errors. setup.lua and settings_page.lua send objects of only model/base_url/type/api_key_env (fine), catalog codex-app sets only .reasoning_effort.
  - **Benefit:** low — ~35 net lines, validate is correct today.
  - **Call:** revise — skip the options macro; for validate, only loop over the known provider keys (ignore unknown ones), keep object-arm null rejection or consciously accept null, keep the cross-field order and messages the tests pin, and add a test for unknown-key objects; otherwise skip.
- [ ] 5.18 Core: per-field empty-table fixups ad hoc (`runtime.rs:825`, `scripting.rs:1577`); default tool params JSON ×2; settings.json read 4–5× per load; `apply_saved_providers` bypasses injected env; plugin errors round-trip via Lua globals; `dispatch` repeats decode; generic `list()` helper lives in agent.rs; attachment name sanitising ×2; MCP state stringly typed; `compact.rs:516` instruction built by splitting PROMPT.
  - **Verdict:** partly — most sub-items hold; a few are minor. Confirmed: empty-table fixups (`runtime.rs:825 message()` and `scripting.rs:1577 compact`), default tool params JSON (`json!({"type":"object","properties":{}})` at mcp.rs:503, runtime.rs:286, scripting.rs:1517), settings read via `settings::load` at lib.rs:371/448/526, scripting.rs:308/1372, attachment name filter (attachments.rs:90 and :164), `apply_saved_providers` reading `load_secrets(dir)`/`saved_settings(dir)` straight from disk instead of the `Source`/env, MCP state as strings (mcp.rs `Status::name()` -> proto `state: String` -> health.rs matches `"ready"`/`"starting"`), compact.rs:516 `PROMPT.split_once(". ")`. Not checked in depth: plugin errors via Lua globals, `dispatch` decode repetition.
  - **Fix:** (1) Empty tables: add one serde helper `de::list_or_empty` (the body of `agent::list`) as `deserialize_with` on `ChatMessage.images`, `tool_calls`, `reasoning_state.items`, and make `CompactConfig` deserialize `[]` as default; delete `runtime::message()` and the compact special-case, and move `agent::list` out of agent.rs. (2) `tools::no_params()` returning the empty schema, used in the 4 places. (3) Attachments: `fn clean_name(&str) -> String`, used at both sites. (4) MCP: a `McpState` enum in bone-proto (serde lowercase, wire-identical) replacing the string in `McpServerInfo.state`; health.rs matches the enum. (5) compact.rs: split PROMPT into `const PROMPT_TASK` + `PROMPT_HOW` (or write the in-place instruction as its own const) instead of splitting at ". " at runtime. (6) Settings: load once per `load_config` and pass the `Value` to `apply_saved_providers`/secrets (this also fixes the injected-env bypass); lib.rs readers go through `settings::load` already, leave them.
  - **Lines:** about -50/+30.
  - **Risk:** medium — sub-items have very different risk. (1) Putting `deserialize_with` on `ChatMessage`: ChatMessage lives in bone-proto (wire type shared with bone-android, an internally tagged enum, goldens in bone-proto/tests/golden.rs); embedding Lua's `{}`-is-a-list quirk in the wire contract is a layering leak and the same hack would be needed for `reasoning_state.items`; also message() applies `list` only for present top-level keys and its "bad message: {e}" prefix is relied on. CompactConfig is `#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]`; an empty seq may already deserialize via visit_seq, verify before special-casing. (2) no_params(): safe, 3 sites (mcp.rs:503, runtime.rs:286, scripting.rs:1517), same literal. (3) clean_name: the two sites differ: attachments.rs:90 falls back to "Screenshot" when blank, :164 keeps a blank name; the helper must only share filter+take(120). (4) McpState enum in bone-proto: strict enum fails the whole mcp/list on an unknown state from a newer/older core (needs `#[serde(other)]`), touches bone-proto golden test and mcp/tests.rs:174,479 (`state == "ready"`/"idle"), and bone-android shares the proto; catalog plugins/mcp/ui.lua reads state strings from mcp/list (127, 776). (5) PROMPT split: must produce byte-identical PROMPT (it is sent to models and prompt-cached); const concat needs concat!/macro; compact in-place text is built from the rest after the first ". ". (6) Settings load once: `apply_saved_providers` runs on the separate bone-lua thread inside load_with_options while `resolve`/`chosen_provider` read settings on the caller thread, so passing a Value means a LoadOptions/lua_thread signature change (LoadOptions is public, Clone, used by tests); the "injected-env bypass" fix is a behaviour change (api_key_env currently read from the real env via std::env::var; `env` closure is `&dyn Fn`, not Send, cannot cross into the Lua thread without pre-resolving). bone.settings (`_settings`, scripting.rs:1372) must keep reading live.
  - **Benefit:** medium — (2),(3),(5) tidy; (1),(4),(6) are riskier than their value.
  - **Call:** revise — do (2) no_params and (3) clean_name (keep the "Screenshot" fallback at the first site) and optionally (5); keep the empty-list tolerance in bone-core (runtime::message) instead of bone-proto; skip (4) McpState (or add serde(other) and update tests, coordinate with android) and defer (6).
- [ ] 5.23 `bone/src/main.rs:190` — `--clipboard-read` parsed from raw argv; test duplicates the private env list.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — main.rs:190 looks at `args_os` by hand before `parse_args`, and the test (main.rs:591) repeats the nine-name env list of `private_core` (main.rs:293).
  - **Fix:** Add a `Mode::ClipboardRead` arm (`"--clipboard-read" => ...`, left out of `USAGE` as it is internal) in `parse_args` and handle it first in `main`, so there is one argv parser. Hoist the list to `const PRIVATE_ENV: [&str; 9]` used by `private_core` and iterated by the test (architecture.md lists the same names; the test then guards the const). Keep the rule that the helper runs before `config_dir`/runtime setup.
  - **Lines:** about -18/+8.
  - **Risk:** low — (a) the raw check uses `args_os` and exact `len()==2`, while `parse_args(std::env::args())` panics on non-UTF-8 args and would let `--clipboard-read` combine with other flags (e.g. `--clipboard-read --headless`); a Mode arm must be exact-one-arg-only, or keep the early args_os check, and be handled before config_dir/write_docs/runtime (and before the `Mode::Gui` request_window path). Spawn site is bone-ui/src/clipboard.rs:33 (cmd.arg("--clipboard-read") only), no other users; USAGE exclusion fine. (b) hoisting the env list to a const used by the test makes the test tautological: removing a name from the const would no longer fail, defeating the pin ("the test then guards the const" is wrong); keep the literal list in the test.
  - **Benefit:** low — removes 7 lines of argv special-casing.
  - **Call:** revise — add `PRIVATE_ENV` const for private_core only if the test keeps its own literal list; leave the --clipboard-read early check as is (or a one-line `is_clipboard_helper(args_os)` helper) rather than routing through parse_args.

## P5 — skipped or not a bug

Call was skip (not worth the risk) or n/a (rejected, leave, keep). Kept so they aren't re-raised.

- [-] 1.8 [checked] `plugins/files/lua/files/html.lua:43` — `string.char(n)` errors for any `&#N;` above 255 (e.g. `&#8212;`); 128–255 give invalid UTF-8. Use a UTF-8 encoder (pdf.lua has `utf8_char`). **Dropped:** the stray bone3 copy of the files plugin was deleted; the catalog copy is the live one.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `string.char(tonumber(n))` errors for n > 255 and emits invalid UTF-8 for 128-255 (LuaJIT has no `utf8.char`).
  - **Fix:** move pdf.lua's `utf8_char` (pdf.lua:652-666) to `bone.util.utf8_char` (shared via bone-lua, available in core and GUI states) and have pdf.lua use `local utf8_char = bone.util.utf8_char`; html.lua calls it from the entity decoder (see 1.9), also accepting hex `&#x...;`. Net roughly zero lines, removes the private copy.
  - **Lines:** about -16/+18
  - **Risk:** medium — target is stale. bone3/plugins/files is an old fork (no platform.lua, a different core.lua and html.lua). The catalog copy ~/projects/bone-catalog/plugins/files/lua/files/html.lua already has its own utf8_char and a one-pass decode. Moving the helper to bone.util.utf8_char makes catalog plugins depend on a newer bone (nil call on older cores). Catalog's own utf8_char has no range guard: `&#99999999;` still errors in string.char.
  - **Benefit:** low — fixes dead or stale code; the real copy needs a range guard (n > 0x10FFFF -> "?").
  - **Call:** skip — do not edit the bone3 fork; if anything, add `if n > 0x10FFFF then return "?" end` to the catalog html.lua utf8_char. Or delete or resync the stale bone3 plugins/files copy.
- [-] 1.9 [checked] `plugins/files/lua/files/html.lua:39` — named entities applied in `pairs` order, so `&amp;lt;` may decode twice. One-pass `gsub("&(#?%w+);", lookup)`. **Dropped:** the stray bone3 copy of the files plugin was deleted; the catalog copy is the live one.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — html.lua:39 iterates `pairs(ENTITIES)`, so `&amp;` may run before `&lt;` and `&amp;lt;` decodes twice.
  - **Fix:** key ENTITIES without `&`/`;` (`amp = "&"`, `lt = "<"` …) and decode in one pass: `text = text:gsub("&(#?%w+);", function(e) local n = e:match("^#(%d+)$") or (e:match("^#[xX](%x+)$") and tonumber(e:match("%x+$"), 16)); if n then return bone.util.utf8_char(tonumber(n)) end return ENTITIES[e] end)` (nil return keeps the text as is). Replaces both the `pairs` loop and the `&#N;` gsub, fixing 1.8 in the same edit.
  - **Lines:** about -7/+8
  - **Risk:** low — the same stale bone3 copy. The catalog html.lua M.decode already does a single gsub pass with a NAMED table, so the double decode is already fixed there. Within the proposal itself the regex and the hex handling check out (`%x+$` on "#x41" gives "41"), but it depends on bone.util.utf8_char (see 1.8).
  - **Benefit:** low — nothing user-visible changes if the catalog copy is the one shipped.
  - **Call:** skip — already fixed in the catalog; resync the bone3 copy from the catalog instead of patching it.
- [ ] 1.17 [checked] `crates/bone-ui/src/lua.rs:1958` — `execute()` handles `"model"` before user commands; a user `/model` command is unreachable. Register /model as a normal command or reject reserved names.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `execute` short-circuits `word == "model"` (lua.rs:1958) before user commands, even though commands.lua:366 already registers a no-op `/model` for help and completion, so a user `bone.cmd` override can never run.
  - **Fix:** implement /model in that Lua command and delete the Rust special case: in commands.lua call `bone.request("model/list", { session_id = sid }, ...)` to find `m.current`, notify `model (name)` when `c.args == ""`, else `bone.request("settings/set", { path = "providers."..cur.name..".model", value = c.args, session_id = sid }, ...)`; remove the `if word == "model"` lines and `model_command` (lua.rs:1997-2030) from bone-ui. `request_typed` stays (processes.rs uses it).
  - **Lines:** about -38/+17
  - **Risk:** medium — moves /model into Lua, changing the message channel (h.info/h.error become bone.notify), error text and the async request path for both TUI and GUI; the no-op registration in commands.lua becomes real; other `execute("model …")` callers (bone.cmd) go through the Lua path. No tests pin /model. The proposal also drops about 35 lines of Rust that currently works.
  - **Benefit:** low — only helps someone who overrides /model with their own bone.cmd; nobody does.
  - **Call:** skip — low value for a cross-frontend rewrite; if wanted, instead have execute() prefer a user command whose callback is not the builtin placeholder.
- [ ] 1.20 [unverified] `crates/bone-gui/src/nodeview/edit.rs:550` — Up/Down/Home/End move by logical lines, not wrapped rows; no sticky goal column.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — Up/Down (edit.rs:550-566) and Home/End use `line_start/line_end` on logical lines, with no goal column; `Shaped.rows` (with `start` and per-char `xs`) already exists and mouse hit-testing uses `shaped.index_at`.
  - **Fix:** for multiline fields, in `field_key` shape the field (`self.field_shape(fx, f, look, width)`, passing `area.width()` down through `field_event`), find the row containing the cursor, take x from `row.xs`, and move to `shaped.index_at(pos(x, row.y + row.height * 1.5 or row.y - 1))`; Home/End use that row's `start`/`end()`. Skip the sticky goal column unless asked (it needs a stored x in `EditState`). Replaces the 15-line logical-line arm, so roughly size-neutral; low priority.
  - **Lines:** about -17/+20
  - **Risk:** medium — field_shape also inserts IME preedit text, so row.xs and index_at char indices can drift from field indices during composition; password fields shape "•" masks; the needed width must be threaded through field_event; the row.height*1.5 hit-test trick is fragile. No tests cover caret movement.
  - **Benefit:** low — the only multiline text_input in the repo or the catalog is the read-only chat composer (composer.lua:235); every other one is single-line, so Up/Down never matter.
  - **Call:** skip — no live multiline editable input uses it.
- [ ] 1.24 [unverified] `crates/bone-tui/src/app.rs:941` — ctrl+c during the "starting" window (turn not yet set) arms quit; a second press quits while the turn starts.
  - **Verdict:** partly — real but minor. While `starting` is true `turn` is None, so `interrupt()` (app.rs:935) skips the cancel branch and arms/executes quit. `turn/cancel` cannot help here since no turn exists yet, and the core's `turn_cancel` is a no-op without an active turn.
  - **Fix:** at the top of `interrupt()`: `if self.chats[self.current].starting && self.chats[self.current].turn.is_none() { return self.info("a turn is starting…"); }`. This keeps ctrl+c from arming quit in the starting window. `starting` is always cleared by the TurnStart/SessionCreate reply or error, so it cannot stick.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+3
  - **Risk:** medium — the early return blocks ctrl+c from quitting for the whole `starting` window, and turn/start "waits on hooks and MCP" (app.rs:1033 comment), which can be long or hang; user can no longer double-ctrl+c out (also skips clear-prompt path, harmless). Nothing cancels in that window, so ctrl+c becomes a dead key with only an info line.
  - **Benefit:** low — avoids an accidental quit during a short window.
  - **Call:** skip — loses the only escape hatch for a stuck start; if wanted, only skip arming quit (still allow second press) rather than blocking.
- [ ] 1.27 [unverified] `crates/bone-tui/src/app.rs:608` — a large paste into a background chat's draft bypasses the placeholder token; the epoch-mismatch branch drops text silently.
  - **Verdict:** partly — nothing is lost on the background branch: the full text is appended to `draft.text` (app.rs:618), only the placeholder token is skipped (cosmetic: a long raw text shows when you switch back). The epoch-mismatch drop is correct: that draft was already sent or cleared, so the text has nowhere to go.
  - **Fix:** none needed. If the cosmetic gap matters, move the token logic from `paste()` (app.rs:768-775) into a small `Composer::paste_into(text, &mut String, &mut Vec<(String,String)>)` and call it from both sites (draft has a `pastes` field), about +6/-6. Recommend skipping.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+0 (skip)
  - **Call:** n/a (verdict partly, fix none/skip)
- [ ] 1.28 [unverified] `crates/bone-ui/src/keys.rs:72` — SHIFT stripped from char keys, so `"shift+a"` binds plain `a` (may be intended — then document "write A").
  - **Verdict:** confirmed as behavior — `Key::new` folds SHIFT out of chars and `parse` keeps the case of the key char, so `"shift+a"` parses to plain `a` while a terminal sends `A` for shift+a; the binding fires on the wrong key. No shipped binding uses `shift+<letter>`.
  - **Fix:** in `parse` (keys.rs:~160), in the single-char branch use the uppercase for shift without ctrl: `let c = raw.chars().last().unwrap(); KeyCode::Char(if mods.contains(KeyModifiers::SHIFT) && !mods.contains(KeyModifiers::CONTROL) { c.to_ascii_uppercase() } else { c })`. (shift+digit has no portable form; document "write the symbol" in the doc comment.)
  - **Lines:** about -1/+4
  - **Risk:** medium — GUI bone_key (bone-gui/src/backend/input.rs:200) lowercases chars and Key::new strips shift, so GUI shift+a is already plain `a`; with the fix `"shift+a"` parses to `A` and never matches in the GUI (today it matches there, and wrongly also on plain `a`). TUI/GUI would diverge. Terms like ctrl+shift+letter unchanged (excluded). No shipped binding or bone-catalog plugin uses `shift+<letter>` (only `ctrl+shift+*` string compares, unaffected by parse).
  - **Benefit:** low — nobody binds it.
  - **Call:** skip — document "write A for shift+a" in the keys docs instead; if fixed, fix GUI key mapping too.
- [ ] 1.34 [unverified] `crates/bone-ui/src/chat.rs:338` — UI derives session titles itself with different limits than core `title_of` (session.rs:936); use the core's title.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed (minor) — the UI `turn_started_with_images` (chat.rs:338) re-derives the title with `truncate(title, 80)` (80 columns incl. "…", `text::width`), core `title_of` (session.rs:936) cuts at 80 chars then appends "…" (81). Same first-line/trim/image-name rule, different cut, so the title shifts on reload.
  - **Fix:** move the line-pick and cut into one pub fn `title_of(text: &str, images: &[ImageAttachment]) -> Option<String>` in bone-proto (types.rs, next to `SessionInfo.title`); core's `title_of(msg)` becomes a 3-line wrapper (or callers match) and chat.rs:338-344 becomes `self.session.as_mut().unwrap().title = title_of(text, &images)`. Do not drop the UI derivation: the sidebar shows the title live before a reload.
  - **Lines:** about -14/+12
  - **Risk:** medium — adds a pub fn to bone-proto (protocol crate) and a TITLE_CHARS constant move; core title_of tests (session.rs) and index.rs:439 depend on it; UI path must keep the images/empty-text fallback (core returns None for no-line, UI currently yields Some("")). Delta being fixed is 1 char (79+… vs 80+…) and column-vs-char counting for wide text, shown only until reload.
  - **Benefit:** low — cosmetic title shift.
  - **Call:** skip — not worth a new proto API; if wanted, change only chat.rs:344 to the same char-count cut (80 chars then "…") locally.
- [ ] 1.37 [unverified] `runtime/lua/bone/ui/views.lua:64` — streaming `kept` cache keyed without session id; two streaming chats can share lines.
  - **Verdict:** partly — the `kept` key `item.kind .. item.key` (views.lua:64) collides across chats (keys are `e<entry>` per chat, see tool_key at :212), but lines stay correct because the cache is validated against `text` prefix and `width`; the cost is two streaming chats evicting each other's cache (and `kept = {}` on any non-streaming draw).
  - **Fix:** key by session: hoist the session prefix from `tool_key` (views.lua:210-213) above `streamed` as `local function item_key(item) ... end` and use `local id = item_key(item) .. item.kind`; `tool_key` becomes that same function (delete the duplicate).
  - **Lines:** about -2/+3
  - **Risk:** low-medium — adds bone.chat.session() to every streaming reasoning/assistant draw: it builds the full session JSON (items(), turns_of O(N), ~15 fields) per frame per streaming item, in a hot path, and can error outside a view context (tests.rs:3770 notes bone.chat.* is view-only). Lines are already correct (cache validated by prefix+width); only a cache-eviction perf effect with two simultaneously streaming chats (and `kept = {}` on any non-streaming draw already evicts).
  - **Benefit:** low — only two concurrent streaming chats.
  - **Call:** skip — adds per-token cost to fix a rare eviction.

Awaiting the stream task before turn_end/turn/finished makes a slow stream hook (bone.http, bone.sleep, bone.ask in a hook; the Lua coroutine is not cancelled by a cancelled turn until turn_end's grace path) delay or hang turn completion, including for cancelled turns. Also requires Turn.stream to become Option moved out of a borrowed Turn.

  - **Fix:** In `refresh`, tag AUTH_REQUIRED only when `why` starts with "token request: HTTP 4"; in mcp.rs's 401 branch use `match refresh(..).await { Ok(()) => retry, Err(e) if !e.starts_with(AUTH_REQUIRED) => return Err(e), _ => {} }` so a transient failure is a plain error the supervisor retries. - [ ] 2.65 [unverified] `crates/bone-lua/src/sys.rs:280` — Utf8Decoder flushes a trailing partial char after an invalid byte.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed (very rare) — with `[0xFF, 0xE2, 0x82]` the first error has `error_len() == Some(1)`, so `valid = tail.len()` and the incomplete `E2 82` is flushed as U+FFFD instead of held for the next chunk.
  - **Fix:** in `Utf8Decoder::decode` iterate `self.tail.utf8_chunks()`: push `valid()`; for a non-empty `invalid()` push U+FFFD unless `std::str::from_utf8(bad).is_err_and(|e| e.error_len().is_none())` (an incomplete tail), in which case hold its length back; then `drain` the consumed prefix. Replaces the current valid-up-to match, roughly line-neutral.
  - **Lines:** about -8/+9
  - **Risk:** low-medium — the proposed utf8_chunks loop must treat "incomplete tail" only for the LAST chunk; a mid-buffer invalid chunk like [E2 82] followed by 'A' also has error_len()==None in isolation and would be wrongly held back/ drained (corrupting output). Decoder is used only by shell.rs:619 and bone-ui/jobs.rs:129.
  - **Benefit:** low — one spurious U+FFFD in a very rare invalid-byte-then-split-char case.
  - **Call:** skip — not worth a rewrite; if wanted, loop on from_utf8 errors with error_len Some(n) (emit one U+FFFD, advance n) and hold back only when error_len() is None.

  - **Fix:** delete the dirty probe: `let commit = git(&["rev-parse", "--short", "HEAD"]).unwrap_or_default();` and fix the lib.rs:138 doc (drop "`-dirty` with local changes"). Cargo cannot watch the worktree cheaply, so the alternative is to keep it and accept staleness. Note the HEAD comment ("when HEAD moves") is only true for branch switches; commits are caught by `index`.

  - **Risk:** low — only informational: update.lua:25 strips `-dirty`; `bone --version` and bug reports show it. Removing the probe drops useful info for developers (the repo's own notes cite "870d5ff1-dirty"), while the staleness bug is cosmetic and one-directional.
  - **Benefit:** low — avoids a sometimes-wrong suffix.
  - **Call:** skip — keep the probe; at most fix the stale doc comment about "HEAD moves" and accept staleness.

  - **Fix:** Add `fn disown<H: Host>(h, what: &Owned)` next to `own` (`if let Some(o) = owner { owned.get_mut(o).map(|v| v.retain(|w| w != what)) }`; Owned already derives PartialEq) and call it in popup_close, `off`, close_panel and where a job is removed. The cost is touching four close paths (popup_close, off, close_panel, job finish) and job completion is in jobs.rs outside any Lua call. The owner at close can differ from the creator, so the scan has to go over all owners.

  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `display_env` runs `tmux show-environment` via `.output()` with no timeout; a wedged tmux server blocks the UI thread on every clipboard paste.
  - **Fix:** Smallest: run the `.output()` in a spawned thread that sends over an mpsc channel and `rx.recv_timeout(Duration::from_millis(500)).ok().and_then(Result::ok)`. If a wedged tmux is judged too unlikely, leave as is and mark won't-fix.
  - **Lines:** about -0/+7

A thread plus recv_timeout still leaks a blocked thread and an unreaped tmux child.
  - **Benefit:** low — a wedged tmux server is rare, and this only delays one paste by one worker's time.
  - **Call:** skip — if wanted, make the clipboard-read path use `tokio::process::Command` under `tokio::time::timeout` and keep the sync display_env for selection.rs.
- [ ] 2.85 [checked] `crates/bone-gui/src/flex.rs:214` — no freeze loop: space freed by min/max clamps is not redistributed.
  - **Verdict:** confirmed — `flex_sizes` is a single pass: when `clamp(size + delta, min, max)` bites, the freed or missing space is never redistributed to the other items (CSS freeze loop).
  - **Fix:** Smallest version: loop at most `items.len()` times; keep `frozen: Vec<bool>`; each round compute free/grow/shrink over unfrozen items only (frozen contribute their clamped size + margin to `used`), then freeze every item whose clamp changed the value and repeat until none changed. Rewrite of the body of flex_sizes, ~15 lines more. Reasonable to defer unless a layout shows it (nothing uses min/max with grow in Lua defaults that I found).
  - **Lines:** about -4/+18
  - **Risk:** high — a freeze loop changes sizes in every layout where a min/max clamp bites while siblings grow or shrink. max_width is used widely in runtime Lua (views.lua, tray.lua, overlays.lua, composer, settings, sidebar) and in catalog plugins (usage, toolstrip, windows, ask_user). Those layouts would re-flow in the approved v6 desktop look, and nothing pins them (flex.rs has 15 tests; none cover this). crates/bone-gui is Rust-owned layout, so per the memory rule, ask before touching it.
  - **Benefit:** low — correct CSS semantics, but no current layout visibly needs it.
  - **Call:** skip — defer, as the review itself suggests, until a layout shows the problem. If done later, take full-app screenshots before and after.

  - **Verdict:** confirmed — setup.lua:34-40 names codex / claude-code / grok, their plugin ids and login commands in the standard library.
  - **Fix:** Declare a `preset` object ({title, login, group}) in each plugin's manifest.json. The catalog already passes entries through, so gen-index.py must copy that one field. In setup.lua, drop the 3 hard-coded rows and make `load_packages`/open call `catalog.index` and append `{title=e.preset.title, name=e.provider or e.name, plugin=e.name, login=e.preset.login, group=...}` to `M.PRESETS` for entries that have `preset` (desktop/setup.lua reads `setup.PRESETS` at view time, so it picks them up). Cost: offline setup loses these rows, which is acceptable because installing them needs the catalog anyway. This is the only fix with a real code increase. If you consider it a deliberate onboarding feature, leaving it is defensible.

  - **Risk:** medium — setup.lua M.PRESETS is indexed by position everywhere (rows() line 145, on_key 480 `M.PRESETS[row.preset]`, 656/678, desktop/setup.lua:63 `presets = setup.PRESETS`). Appending catalog-derived rows asynchronously after catalog.index returns (a) puts them after the "other" row so the "a plan you already pay for" group is split/misordered unless inserted before it, (b) shifts rows and `st.sel` under the user mid-selection, (c) makes the provider step depend on network: offline / first-run (the exact moment setup auto-opens) loses Codex/Claude Code/Grok rows, and /setup starts hitting the catalog URL on open instead of only on the packages step. Also needs manifest schema + gen-index.py + check.py changes in a second repo and a min_bone_version for the new field.
  - **Benefit:** low — removes 3 name literals from a default-Lua file (allowed by the rules: defaults are Lua), at a net code increase.
  - **Call:** skip — deliberate onboarding feature; offline degradation and index-shifting are worse than the three literals.

  - **Verdict:** partly — the hex are definitions of theme groups (NewChatDot1-10, NewChatTitle, NewChatHint) in a Lua file, not hard-coded at use sites. But no colorscheme can restyle them.

It needs a guard because ansi.lua defines no groups, and the undefined dots would render in the default foreground: `if not bone.hl.get("NewChatDot1") then return {} end` in the render function. The proposed guard `return {}` then makes the empty chat render nothing (no hint text at all), a visible regression; without the guard the dots render in default fg (bright field).   - **Fix:**
  - `prune` (shell.rs:457): runs once per finished job, n <= MAX_COMPLETED (64), so `remove(0)` is trivial. The "global cap" (completed jobs evicted across owners) is a behaviour question, not performance.
  - `Cleaner` `\r` (shell.rs:889): `out.rfind('\n')` only scans back to the last newline, which is short for progress bars. It is quadratic only for one multi-MB line of `\r` redraws (pathological), and the buffer is capped by `trim_front`/MAX_OUTPUT. Leave.
  - `edit_file.rs:114`: the char_indices + `starts_with` scan is linear with a tiny constant (first-byte mismatch), once per edit call. `match_indices` would be shorter but changes how overlapping matches count, so it isn't worth the behaviour risk. Leave.
  - `read_file.rs:67`: reads at most MAX_IMAGE_BYTES (20 MiB) in a single call, and counting lines for the "N lines not shown" total needs the whole text anyway. Leave.
  - **Lines:** 0
  - **Risk:** n/a
  - **Benefit:** n/a
  - **Call:** n/a (verdict leave)

  - **Fix:** before changing anything, run once with the real window and the existing `profile::Span`s (`text-measure`, `text-paint`, `place`). Only if `text-measure` + `text-paint` show up, add ONE memo inside `GpuiShaper::shape` (cache `Arc<Shaped>` by `Job`, dropped at frame end). That covers measure vs paint, `metrics`, the prompt and canvas re-shaping together, with no invalidation logic (the key is the job itself). Everything else, per sub-point:
  - `flex::place` over the whole tree each frame: needed for hit-testing and scroll, and measured cheap. Leave.
  - `Field::chars()`, `apply_fonts` tuple clones (view.rs:185), `keys.rs:238` events clone, `popups.rs:101` clone: per key or per frame, but a handful of small allocations. Leave.
  - `preview.rs:93` key clones (a few previews) and `preview.rs:160` double decode (once per image, off-thread, `normalize` also applies the bounds): leave.
  - `native.rs:414` BGRA clone: inside `or_insert_with`, so once per picture id, not per frame. Leave.
  - `list.rs:245` linear scan: ~4000 f32 adds per frame (about 4us). A prefix sum would need invalidation. Leave.
  - `edits`/`focused_once` never pruned: a few small strings per distinct node key. Leave.
  - `node.rs` Props scans: run only on a rebuild (dirty), over ~5-10 named fields per node, already a single Lua pass. Leave.
  - Prompt draft split/shape (paint.rs:834): one short string per frame. Leave.
  - **Lines:** 0 (about +20 only if profiling justifies the memo)
  - **Risk:** n/a
  - **Benefit:** n/a
  - **Call:** n/a (verdict leave)
- [ ] 6.14 [unverified] Both frontends poll Lua files every 250 ms walking all dirs (`bone-gui/src/lib.rs:641`, `bone-tui/src/lib.rs:337`). Use a watcher or poll dir mtimes.
  - **Verdict:** leave — real but small. The GUI does the walk on a `spawn_blocking` thread and the TUI inline, both every 250 ms: about 100 stats plus a few `read_dir`s (this machine has 108 `.lua` files), roughly 0.3-1 ms per tick, well under 1% CPU. Both walkers are deliberately dependency-free (see the comment in lib.rs:331), and a watcher crate would break the std-first rule. Polling dir mtimes would miss in-place edits, which is exactly what the hash of file mtimes catches.
  - **Fix:** none. If idle wakeups matter, raise `CONFIG_POLL` / `LUA_RELOAD_POLL` to 1 s (a one-constant change, loses some live-reload snappiness).
  - **Lines:** 0
  - **Risk:** n/a
  - **Benefit:** n/a
  - **Call:** n/a (verdict leave)
- [ ] 6.17 [unverified] `runtime/lua/bone/update.lua:24` — `git fetch` every startup (source builds), no throttle.
  - **Verdict:** leave — runs once per process in the background (async `bone.system`, 30 s timeout), only for source builds, and the user's own checkout is the audience. It costs one network fetch per launch, not UI time. A throttle needs a persisted timestamp (`bone.state`) plus a branch (~6 lines) for a non-problem; the same `check()` also does the catalog fetch every start by design.
  - **Fix:** none. If wanted later: store the last-check time via `bone.state` and skip `behind()` within a few hours.
  - **Lines:** 0
  - **Risk:** n/a
  - **Benefit:** n/a
  - **Call:** n/a (verdict leave)

## Heads-up

- `Cargo.toml` gained a `gpui` patch to `crates/bone-gui/vendor/gpui/`, which is untracked. Commit them together or a clean checkout won't build.
